## 4. Kernel-by-kernel plan

In build order. Each entry: the equations, the current implementation to read first, what changes, and the case the item lands with. Every item lands with its dense-AD containment (the configurations of §6.1 item 11 at 6 and 8 cells) before the next starts, as in Phase 1.

### 4.1 The correction (Closures)

*Equations.* §3.2 of this plan; the balance row of cell `k` becomes `Σ_f A_f (w·n)_f − (θ_k − θ_A) V_k − s_k = 0` with `s_k = Σ_f A_f (c·n)_f`, so `w − c` satisfies the volume balance and is the solid velocity; the pinned node's row `w_p = 0`; a Lagrangian node has `c = 0` and no row of its own (its velocity follows the balance rows as now). *Read first.* `src/Closures/balance.jl:139-200` (`_velocity_rows!`, `_consistent_velocities!`, `_coefficient`, `_sweep_rate`), `src/States/derived.jl` (`CellReference`, `DerivedState`, `derive!`), `src/Meshes/topology.jl` (`lagrangian`), `src/Integration/setup.jl:300-420`. *Changes.* The `Correction` types and `correction!` (§3.1); `ds.c`; `CellReference.thickness`; the sweep term in both velocity functions; `setup` builds the correction from its keywords, takes the layer-interface nodes as an explicit list (§3.9) and passes them to `line_mesh`; `PrescribedCorrection` replaces the `override` field of `BalanceRowClosure` (one path for both; the field is read in `balance.jl`, `pattern.jl` and `checks.jl`, and `test/newcore/test_mesh_motion.jl` constructs a closure with the keyword and changes with it). The round-off check of `_consistent_motion!` (`setup.jl`) gains the sweep in its scale as C52's does. *Lands with.* The unit tests of §6.1 items 1 to 4; V-MMS-02/03/04 and the Phase 1 relative-flux tests unchanged under `PrescribedCorrection` (bit-identical to Phase 1, §7.3 item 5).

### 4.2 Relative fluxes under the correction (Kernels)

*Equations.* §3.3. *Read first.* `src/Kernels/relative_flux.jl` (`_stencil`, `_face_value`, `contribute!`), `test/newcore/test_relative_flux.jl`. *Changes.* `u = −c_f n_f` from `ds.c`; the kernel installed whenever the correction is not `NoCorrection`; the `setup` refusals of an override on a reacting material and of deactivation under an override stay for `PrescribedCorrection` and do not apply to the equidistribution correction. *Lands with.* Isothermal advection under an `EquidistributionCorrection` with reactions off (§6.1 item 5); V-BO-01 at `c ≠ 0` (§6.2).

### 4.3 The block-and-band rule (Deactivation)

*Equations.* §3.4. *Read first.* `src/Deactivation/events.jl` (`deactivate!`, `_transfer!`, `_lose!`, `_mask!`), `test/newcore/test_deactivation.jl` ("every cell below the threshold goes in one pass", "a void under a live cell ends the solve"). *Changes.* The run from the top within the band; the chain of transfers into the cell beneath the run; the survivor rule; the invocation index on `DeactivationEvent`; `replay_events!` unchanged (it reads times). The band parameter is an absolute mass fraction `ε_band ≥ ε_m` (default 0.1), not a multiple of `ε_m`: at `ε_m = 3e-2` a band of ten thresholds took cells at 29 % of their mass (A.4), which is not a near-threshold band. *Lands with.* V-BO-04 re-pinned (C46: one invocation, one survivor, the exact law through the event); the tests of §6.1 items 6 to 8; V-BO-02 and V-CPL-02/02b unchanged (a fixed mesh and a one-cell-at-a-time ablation front never form a block).

### 4.4 The Jacobian pattern (ColoredJacobian)

*Equations.* §3.6. *Read first.* `src/ColoredJacobian/pattern.jl:96-115`, `test/newcore/jacobian/test_pattern.jl`, `test_containment.jl`. *Changes.* The velocity rows' reach under a correction; the color-count formula gains the correction configurations; containment with every node activated (§3.6). *Lands with.* Containment on V-BO-01's configuration (in-depth radiation, three components, moving mesh) and on the V-BO-04 and heterogeneous-layer configurations, at 6 and 8 cells, five states, both energy forms.

### 4.5 Setup, the domain test and the outputs (Integration, Outputs)

*Read first.* `src/Integration/setup.jl` (`_require_supported`, `setup`), `checks.jl` (`constraint_residual`), `solve.jl` (`_deactivation_callback`, `_observe!`), `src/Outputs/solution.jl` (`CoreExtras`, `SolverStatistics`). *Changes.* Keywords `correction`, `lagrangian`, `activation`, `band`, `switch_width`, `located_events`; the sweep in the constraint scale (C52); `correction_velocity` and `min_cell_thickness` series, `invocations` in the statistics; the trial clamp in the surface Newton (C36: the iterate is clamped smoothly into the property bracket as every other temperature-sensitive kernel's input is, the warnings removed, the old core unchanged because the Newton body is shared and the clamp is a keyword the new core sets). *Lands with.* The keywords and outputs at M3; V-BO-01 and V-BO-03 are routed at M4 (§6.2, §7); C36 lands at M1 with its test (a wild trial state runs the Newton without a warning and with finite derivatives on the new core; the old core keeps its clamp and warnings).

### 4.6 Event location (Integration)

*Equations.* §3.5. *Read first.* `solve.jl:378-420`, the prototype's continuous variant (A.5), SciMLBase `ContinuousCallback` (`affect_neg!`, `RightRootFind`). *Changes.* Behind `located_events`; the discrete safety net kept; the nudge at the root; the measurement of §7.3 item 7 decides the default. *Lands with.* §6.1 items 9 and 10; V-BO-02 at both tolerances (the event-time shift as a QoI of the gate script).

### 4.7 What is not built

The slot-shifting interior merge (C54), the Péclet weighting of the Darcy upwind (C21: the default integrator has no Newton to fail at the kink, and the moving-mesh decks of Phase 2 do not reverse their flow; left to the owner's call), a centred face temperature for the relative energy flux (C33: kept upwind-biased), the monitor's reaction-rate term `γ` (design §3.3 marks it optional; the normalized mass term already acts only on mixed compositions, §3.2), and any change to the energy forms.

---

## 5. Solver plan

### 5.1 The integrator and its policies

`Rodas5P` with the solver-chosen restart stays the default (C28, C40). On the event-dense fixed-mesh deck the alternatives measured in this session (A.6): the forced restart (`DT_AFTER_EVENT = 1e-6` s) takes 2,790 accepted steps against 1,706, 30 % more wall time, and cuts the ledger quantity of C39 from 1.5e-3 to 7.6e-5 (the post-event gas transient is then resolved); `KenCarp4` with the raw estimate takes 10,021 steps, 1,784 nonlinear failures and 3.75× the time. The restart policy is therefore a gated choice for deactivating solves (§10): the forced restart buys the old core's ledger tolerance back at a measured price.

### 5.2 Event location

§3.5 and §4.6. The continuous callback's cost is 2 to 4 % of the solve; its benefit is the event time tied to the tolerance (shift 0.022 of the median interval between reltol 1e-6 and 1e-8, against 0.133 for the discrete callback). The gate item 8 of Phase 1 (achieved error against requested tolerance) is re-run on the deactivating cases with location on and off (§7.3 item 7); the default follows.

### 5.3 The constraint check and the domain test

C52's scale; `MAX_CONSTRAINT_REJECTIONS` unchanged; the largest accepted residual and the domain rejections per solve are gate quantities (the prototype's 0.69 and 52 of 1,091 proposals on V-BO-01 are the numbers to beat, A.3).

### 5.4 Minimum cell size

The design asks Phase 2 to measure, not assume, the smallest cell the correction produces. Measured on the prototype (A.3, A.4): 1.0 µm at 60 PMMA cells, 2.0 µm at 30, 0.49 µm at 120, 0.31 µm at `ε_m = 3e-3`, all about `ε_m`-to-`ε_band` of the initial cell, which is the design's "bounded relative to the layer": the layer's cells shrink with the layer until the band takes them. No step fell below 2e-5 s and no solve stalled; the conduction coefficient `k/h²` of a micrometre cell is 2e11 W m⁻³ K⁻¹ and `StructuredKLU` factored every iteration matrix. The `min_thickness` termination (D18) measures the whole slab and does not bind. The gate records `min_cell_thickness` on every run (§7.3 item 9).

### 5.5 Wall time: where the prototype's cost is, and the levers

At 60 PMMA cells the prototype takes 2.8 to 3.5 s against the old core's 0.58 to 0.80 s (A.3: 3.5 to 6×), at 120 cells 9.3 s against 2.1 s and at 30 cells 1.46 against 0.25 s (A.4; the old core's times are A.2's medians from another process, so the ratios carry the machine's spread); V-BO-02, which has no correction, takes 1.32 s against 1.29 s in one process (A.1). The correction's cost has three measured parts:

1. **A Jacobian every step at 30 colors.** `Rodas5P` builds 1,091 Jacobians over 1,091 accepted steps, where the old core built 164 over 853; the correction's pattern colors in 30 against 18 at `c = 0` (C43), so one Jacobian costs about 1.7× more. Roughly half of the solve is Jacobians. A Rosenbrock-W method would allow reuse, but none of the installed W-methods (`ROS34PW2`, `Rodas23W`) reaches the old core's accuracy within 3× of its time even on the smooth audit decks (A.7), and OrdinaryDiffEq rebuilds their Jacobian every step as well; `KenCarp4`, which reuses its Jacobian, is 3.75× slower than `Rodas5P` on the event-dense deck (A.6). The integrator's accuracy per tolerance, Phase 1's open lever, does not close on the moving-mesh deck with any candidate (A.7: the best, `Rodas5P` at reltol 3e-7, is 1.14× the old core's error at 1.52× its time; `Rodas5Pe` at 1e-7 is 0.28× at 1.77×). The tolerance weights of the algebraic rows are probed in A.9.
2. **Rejected steps.** 246 of 1,337 proposals, 52 of them by the domain test on the constraint residual (C52 is expected to remove most of those).
3. **The steps themselves.** 1,091 against 853: the correction adds stiffness at `τ = 0.2` s (at `τ = 1`, 691 steps; at `τ = 0.1`, 1,286).

The gate's wall-time clause is measured at matched accuracy (the Phase 1 protocol: a run's error is its largest temperature difference against the same core at reltol 1e-11) on V-BO-01, 03 and 04 at 30, 60 and 120 cells, both cores in one session, three repeats; the result is reported as a ratio with the machine's spread, and the owner decides whether C40's exception extends to it (question 6). The plan does not promise parity: the prototype is 3.5 to 6× at equal tolerance before C52 and before any tuning of `τ`, matched accuracy raises the ratio, and the levers above do not reach 2×; the honest expectation is 4× or more, and whether any ratio is accepted is question 6.

### 5.6 Energy form

The temperature form stays the default; the enthalpy form reproduces every V-BO-01 quantity with the correction at 3.1 to 3.9× the temperature form's time (A.4: 10.9 s against 2.8 to 3.5 s), consistent with Phase 1's 2 to 13×. The gate runs the V-BO ladder in both forms (§7.3 item 3).

### 5.7 Performance: why the burnout path is slower, and the milestone that must close it

**The owner's rule.** The new core must run the burnout path within the same order of wall time as the old core at matched accuracy, or faster; a path that cannot is a bad sign for the architecture, not a detail to carry. Milestone M2 exists to settle that before the correction is built, with a stop at its gate: within 1.5× passes, 1.5 to 2× is the owner's call, above 2× the phase stops for the owner's decision (continue with the levers below, ship `c = 0` with the fallback, or reconsider the cutover itself).

**Where the time goes** (V-BO-01 at 60 PMMA cells, equal tolerance; A.2, A.3):

| | old core | new core with the correction |
|---|---|---|
| accepted steps (rejected) | 853 (3) | 1,091 (246) |
| Jacobians | 164, the reduced approximate one, reused across steps | 1,091, exact, 30 colors (about 15 to 25 residual-equivalents each) |
| right-hand sides | 19,076 | 11,787, plus 1,337 residuals for the domain test |
| residual cost | 1 | 1.15 to 1.54 (Phase 1 leftover 1) |
| wall | 0.58 to 0.80 s | 2.8 to 3.5 s |

Four causes, none intrinsic to the design: (1) `Rodas5P` needs an exact Jacobian every step by construction, about half the solve, where `KenCarp4` on the old core reuses an approximate one over about five steps; it was chosen because the Newton-based candidates behaved badly on the new residual (`KenCarp4` on V-BO-02: 10,021 steps and 1,784 nonlinear failures against the old core's 1,572 and 56; on the Eulerian audit deck its error does not fall with the tolerance, A.7), and that behaviour is undiagnosed; (2) the correction's reconstruction widens the pattern from 18 to 30 colors, 1.7× per Jacobian; (3) more steps and rejections: the correction's stiffness (691, 1,091 and 1,286 steps at `τ_p` of 1, 0.2 and 0.1), an 18 % rejection rate and one residual per proposal for the domain test, and at matched accuracy `Rodas5P`'s reltol 1e-7 to 3e-7 against the old default's 1e-6; (4) the residual itself at 1.15 to 1.54× with no kernel ever profiled or tuned. The old core's path carries ten months of performance work (KLU, the structural diagonal, the ring trim, the reduced Jacobian reused across steps, the step reset after a park); the new core has had none.

**The levers, in the order the measurements rank them.** (a) Diagnose the Newton-based integrators on the new residual: the suspects are the error weights of the mass rows (`reltol · ε_m · m(0)`, which A.9 shows carry the accuracy), the smoothed estimator on the new variables, the one-sided limiter and smooth floors near depletion, and the restart after events; a reusing integrator that qualifies cuts the Jacobian count five-fold and is the one lever that can reach parity alone. (b) Failing that, Jacobian reuse for a Rosenbrock-W method (`RosenbrockW6S4OS` is fourth order and tolerates an inexact Jacobian); OrdinaryDiffEq rebuilds the Jacobian of its W-methods every step, so this is a small stepper change, upstream or local. (c) The per-Jacobian cost: analytical fast paths for the conduction and gas-transport kernels inside the dual passes (declined in Phase 1 when the Jacobian was a quarter of a solve, worth revisiting at half) and a compact smooth stencil for the relative flux. (d) Steps and rejections: C52, the controller's gains, the largest `τ` the band permits. (e) A kernel performance pass on the residual (the boundary-condition kernels led the Phase 1 profiles). Stacked without (a) these plausibly reach 1.5 to 3×; parity or better needs (a) or (b).

**What is measured.** `burnout_timing.jl` with a cost breakdown per solve (Jacobians, right-hand sides, factorizations, solves, the domain test, the remainder) on V-BO-02, on the prototype's correction run of V-BO-01 (the prototype is enough for M2: the lever is the integrator and the Jacobian, not the correction's code) and on the audit decks, both cores in one process, before and after each lever, so the gate's ratio has its anatomy beside it.


---

## 6. Test and verification plan

### 6.1 New tests (`test/newcore/`)

1. **The correction's zeros and smoothness** (`test_correction.jl`). `c = 0` at the pinned, surface and Lagrangian nodes and wherever both cells are above `r_hi`; the activation is `C¹` in `log(h/h₀)` (finite differences across both joins); the sign moves a node into the thicker cell; at or below zero thickness the activation is one and finite.
2. **Relaxed equidistribution.** On a three-cell inert mesh with every node activated (`r_hi` above one), `M_i h_i` relaxes to a common value with a rate set by `τ`, against the closed form of the linearized recurrence.
3. **The balance rows with the sweep.** After the consistent sweep `w − c` satisfies every cell's volume balance to round-off; the scaled constraint residual (C52) is at round-off; a uniform strain rate with `c ≠ 0` gives `w = v_s + c` exactly on planar, cylindrical and spherical meshes.
4. **`PrescribedCorrection` is the Phase 1 override.** V-MMS-03's trajectory hash under `w_override` equals Phase 1's (bit for bit); `test_relative_flux.jl` unchanged.
5. **Isothermal advection and free stream under an `EquidistributionCorrection`** with reactions off: temperature constant to 1e-9 K, total mass to round-off, a uniform concentration uniform.
6. **The block rule** (`test_deactivation.jl`). Every cell of a column crossing at once ends in one invocation with one survivor whose inventories are the column's; a run of `k` cells under a live cell merges into that cell in one invocation; every inventory conserved through the chain; admissibility after the pass; invocation ids on the events; `park_times` one per cell.
7. **The band.** A cell at 1.5 `ε_m` beneath a top cell at 0.9 `ε_m` merges with it; a cell above the band stays; the band is an absolute fraction (`ε_band = 0.1` takes the same cells at `ε_m = 1e-2` and `3e-3`).
8. **An interior void beneath a healthy skin still ends the solve** (`:interior`).
9. **Event location.** On a two-cell deck the located event time's shift between tolerances falls with the tolerance; the safety net deactivates a cell a root left at the threshold (the nudge); location and the discrete predicate agree on the cell.
10. **A located event's state** is admissible and leaves the velocity and intensity rows at round-off after the sweep.
11. **Containment** with the correction active (every node activated and the perturbed, zero and negative states) on the V-BO-01 (in-depth, three components), V-BO-04 and heterogeneous-layer configurations, at 6 and 8 cells, both forms; colored equal to dense; the color count against the formula.
12. **Trial states.** `sensible_enthalpy` of a non-finite temperature is `NaN` (C47); the relative-flux kernel on a `NaN` state returns a `NaN` residual without throwing; the activation at negative thickness.
13. **The surface Newton's trial clamp** (C36): a wild trial state runs without a warning, with finite values and derivatives; physical states unchanged bit for bit.
14. **Outputs.** `correction_velocity`, `min_cell_thickness`, the events' invocations and the `invocations` statistic; NaN beyond the live cells after a block merge.
15. **Allocations.** Zero bytes per residual and per colored Jacobian with the correction present (the Phase 1 gates extended).
16. **Determinism.** Repeated solves bit-identical with the correction and events, under `StructuredKLU` and UMFPACK.
17. **The fallback, only if built** (C54, §7.4). An interior cell's merge and the slot shift conserve every inventory, shift every per-slot quantity listed in C54, keep the mask contiguous from the top, leave the state admissible and the algebraic rows consistent, and V-BO-01 and V-BO-03 pass their QoIs at `c = 0`.

### 6.2 Verification cases

| Case | Routing on the new core | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| V-BO-01 | routed with the correction (`correction = :equidistribution`, the defaults, `lagrangian` the interface node) | every current QoI at its tolerance (the case's fixed references) at 30, 60 and 120 PMMA cells, the 30-cell row an owner exception if R25 stands; invocations exactly one for the PMMA layer as a new QoI, with the largest merged fraction at most 5 `ε_m`; the surface-Newton warning count replaced by the count of clamped trial iterates once C36 removes the warnings (question 8) |
| V-BO-02 | as now (fixed mesh, no correction) | unchanged; the ledger QoI at C39's 3e-3 unless the restart policy changes (question 7) |
| V-BO-03 | routed with the correction | its four QoIs as pinned (the transmitted-flux warning becomes the `radiation_transmitted` series' peak, the surface-Newton count the clamped-iterate count; owner question 8) |
| V-BO-04 | routed, no correction needed (all cells cross together; the block rule) | re-pinned per C46: one invocation, one survivor, the exact law through the event, isothermal, `ξ_s = ρ_s` on live cells, final outcome |
| V-BO-05 | routed (fixed mesh) | re-pinned per C45: `:interior` at the first `ε_m` crossing beneath a live cell, the stop time from a ladder |
| V-BO-06 (new) | V-BO-01 on a mesh graded toward the surface: `_vbo_problem` gains a grading keyword (a geometric ratio of 1.02 per cell across the 60 PMMA cells, so the surface cell is a third of the interface cell; the Kaowool at the interface pitch), the same materials, conditions and 500 s | one invocation; the V-BO-01 case's fixed references at their tolerances (they are mesh-independent); the old core at the same graded mesh reported beside |
| V-BO-07 (new) | a heterogeneous layer of mixed composition: every PMMA cell also holds a second solid of the same properties but half the density (605 kg m⁻³) and a pre-exponential factor a tenth of PMMA's, with a mass fraction graded linearly from 0 at the interface to 0.5 at the surface, so the density varies inside one activated region and the normalized monitor's mass term acts (two pure sub-layers separated by a Lagrangian node would equidistribute separately and `β` would change nothing at equilibrium); the Kaowool substrate, conditions and 500 s of V-BO-01 | one invocation for the layer; mass loss equal to the layer's initial mass (exact); ledger at 1e-3; `β = 1` against `β = 0` on invocations, merged fractions and MLR; the old core at the same deck reported (peak and timing have no fixed reference until the CI run pins them) |
| V-BO-08 (new) | localized burnout: a 1 mm PMMA layer (ten cells at the V-BO-01 pitch) on the 12 mm substrate, the V-BO-01 materials and conditions | one invocation; `correction_velocity` exactly zero at every Kaowool node on every saved step; the Kaowool temperatures within 1e-10 of the `NoCorrection` run until the first activation; mass loss exact; ledger at 1e-3; the MLR curve against the old core at the same deck |
| V-CPL-02/02b, V-CPL-03, V-MMS-02/03/04 and every Phase 1 case | unchanged routing | bit-identical to Phase 1 with `NoCorrection` (§7.3 item 5) |
| The Douglas fir deck (`examples/07_douglas_fir_cone.jl`, 60 s and 600 s) | a gate script, not a case | bit-identical with `NoCorrection`; to 1e-10 with an inactive correction; wall time recorded on both cores (A.8); the matched-accuracy comparison is Phase 3's (question 9) |

### 6.3 Gate scripts (`test/newcore/gates/`)

`burnout_ladder.jl` (item 1 of §7.3: the matrix of cases, grids, thresholds, monitor settings and `τ`; invocations, outcome, QoIs, wall time, `min_cell_thickness`, constraint residual, domain rejections), `burnout_identity.jl` (item 5: hashes of the charring decks and the Douglas fir deck with `NoCorrection` and with an inactive correction), `burnout_timing.jl` (item 8: both cores at matched accuracy, the Phase 1 protocol, with the cost breakdown of §5.7), `burnout_gradients.jl` (item 9: the Phase 0 `fd_gradients.jl` protocol on the new core), `case_accuracy.jl` extended to the deactivating cases with location on and off (item 7), `energy_ledger.jl` extended to a burnout deck (item 3). Every table stamped with its commit (D52); the measurements under `measurements/`.

---

## 7. Milestones, gates, estimates

Single developer; the old core untouched (except C55 and C36, fingerprint-checked); targeted gates per milestone (plan §9.3, D21); the full three-way `Pkg.test()` at M7. The design estimated 4 to 6 weeks for Phase 2; the estimate below is 11.5 to 14.5, because the burnout path's wall time must be brought within the old core's order first (M2, §5.7), and because the cascade rule, the event location, the three new cases and the gradient re-measurement are concrete items the design listed in one line each, and because the wall-time clause needs the matched-accuracy protocol on a ladder.

| Milestone | Builds | Gate to pass before the next | Estimate |
|---|---|---|---|
| M0 Branch and plan | This plan, the prototype and its tables (§9.2) | Format check, Aqua and JET green locally (the Quality workflow runs only for `main`, §9.1); the owner's answers to questions 1 to 4 gate M1's start, not M0's length | 2 days |
| M1 Deactivation | The block-and-band rule, invocation ids, the survivor and layer-end rules, C47, C36 | §6.1 items 6 to 8, 12, 13; V-BO-04 re-pinned and routed; V-BO-02, V-CPL-02/02b bit-identical; for C36 (an edit of shared old-core code) the fingerprint, `test/solver/test_surface_bc_guards.jl`, `test/boundary_conditions/test_boundary_temperature.jl` and the full old-core `Pkg.test()`, the old core keeping its clamp and warnings (keyword off) | 1 week |
| M2 Performance of the burnout path | The diagnosis and the levers of §5.7, in the order the measurements rank them: why the Newton-based integrators fail on the new residual (the erratic `KenCarp4` error and its nonlinear failures), Jacobian reuse (a qualifying reusing integrator, or a reuse policy for a Rosenbrock-W method), the per-Jacobian cost (fast paths for the conduction and gas-transport kernels in the dual passes, a compact relative-flux stencil), the step and rejection count (C52, the controller), and a kernel performance pass on the residual; `burnout_timing.jl` with its cost breakdown | V-BO-02 (the burnout path without a correction) and the prototype's correction run on V-BO-01 at matched accuracy against the old core, both cores in one process: within 1.5× passes (the same order); 1.5 to 2× is the owner's call; above 2× is the bad sign of §5.7 and the phase stops at this point for the owner's decision | 2 to 3 weeks |
| M3 The correction | `Correction` types, `correction!`, `ds.c`, `CellReference.thickness`, the sweep in the balance rows and the consistent sweep, the Lagrangian nodes, `RelativeFluxKernel` on `ds.c`, the pattern (C43), C52, `setup` keywords, outputs | §6.1 items 1 to 5, 11, 14 to 16; V-MMS-02/03/04 and `test_relative_flux.jl` unchanged under `PrescribedCorrection`; V-BO-01 at 60 cells ends in one invocation with every QoI within its tolerance (the prototype's A.3 run, not within round-off of it: C52 changes the rejected steps) | 2 weeks |
| M4 The ladder | V-BO-01 and V-BO-03 routed; the matrix of §7.3 item 1 at the first thresholds; the end-of-layer tail diagnostic (R25); both energy forms; `burnout_ladder.jl`, `burnout_timing.jl`, the `energy_ledger.jl` extension; `min_cell_thickness`; a tuning allowance of half a week for `τ`, the band and the activation | §7.3 items 1 to 3, 6, 8, 10 on V-BO-01/03/04; the decision point of §7.4 | 2 to 2.5 weeks |
| M5 Events | Event location behind `located_events` (prototyped, A.5), the restart-policy measurement on V-BO-01 | §6.1 items 9, 10; §7.3 item 7; the defaults of questions 5 and 7 chosen | 0.5 week |
| M6a The decision review's changes (§14.3) | The plan records (W2); `burnout_gradients.jl` (W3) and the burst measured per step (W4), each checked on `768944e`; the reactivity switch deleted (W1); the correction installed by the rule (W5) | §14.4's M6a row | 2.5 weeks |
| M6b The gate's cases | V-BO-05 re-pinned from a ladder and routed; V-BO-06/07/08 (a graded mesh with its old-core reference, a new two-solid material, a thin layer); V-BO-03's ladder rows past 500 s | Each case's QoIs in both forms; §6.2's acceptance | 1.5 to 2 weeks |
| MF The `FBDF` switch (§14.3) | On an OrdinaryDiffEqBDF release containing the upstream fix: the compat floor, `FBDF` as the default policy, the gas rows at scale (c), V-MMS-02/03 under `rodas5p_policy`, the re-pins of §14.6 | §14.3's MF list | 1 to 1.5 weeks |
| M6c The measurement pass | Every gate table once, on the final defaults (§14.4) | §7.3 items 1 to 10 | 1 week |
| M7 Gate document | `docs/Mesh_Framework_Phase2_Gate.md`, §12 of this plan, the three-way `Pkg.test()` in detached worktrees | The Phase 2 gate (§7.3) | 1 week |
| Review | The adversarial review of the phase and its followups, as Phase 1 experienced it (D38 to D52, C32 to C39, a re-measured gate) | The review's findings closed or owned | 1 to 2 weeks |

Total: 11.5 to 14.5 weeks as first planned (the design said 4 to 6), 13 to 16 if the fallback of §7.4 is built. The decision review (§14.4) raises it to 13.5 to 17.5 weeks without MF and 14.5 to 19 with it. Phase 1's record is an aggregate (about twelve and a half of thirteen to fourteen weeks, plus an unbudgeted review); its per-milestone actuals were never recorded, so these rows are estimates against the deliverable count (about 25 code-level items in §2.2 and §4, 17 tests, four new gate scripts, three new cases, four re-pins), not against measured velocity.

### 7.1 What the owner decides before M1

1. Question 1 (the interior policy and whether the slot-shifting merge is insurance now or the fallback later).
2. Question 2 (the band as an absolute fraction, its default).
3. Question 4 (thresholds as material parameters).
4. Question 8's V-BO-04 re-pin (M3 needs it).

### 7.2 Design errata to apply at the design's next revision

§3.3: the activation is per node, the smooth "or" of its two cells' switches (C44); §3.5: the cascade is the block-and-band rule, the band an absolute mass fraction (C42); §3.7: the color count with the correction active includes the velocity rows' reach (C43) and the `c = 0` count is `3NC + 7 + 2·bl + 7·χ` (C25); §3.5 and §6 Phase 2: "one event per layer" counts callback invocations (C53); §3.5: the bullet "Reaction rates are multiplied by a smooth function…" and the "smooth reactivity switch-off" of its Phase 2 text go, since the switch is deleted (§14, W1). No design content changes beyond these; the owner applies them.

### 7.3 The Phase 2 gate, as written in the design, made measurable

Every item is measured and reported in `docs/Mesh_Framework_Phase2_Gate.md` with its script and table.

1. **One event per layer or fewer across the ladder.** `burnout_ladder.jl`, table `measurements/burnout_ladder.md`: V-BO-01 at 30, 60 and 120 PMMA cells × `ε_m ∈ {3e-3, 1e-2, 3e-2}` at the default `τ` and `ε_band`, plus `τ ∈ {0.1, 0.2, 1}` at 60 cells and `ε_m = 1e-2`; V-BO-07 with `β ∈ {0, 1}` (the monitor's mass term acts only where the density varies, so the "two monitor settings" are carried by the heterogeneous layer; on V-BO-01 the prototype's `β = 1` was a `τ/2` duplicate, A.4); V-BO-06 and V-BO-08 at their CI resolutions and the three thresholds; V-BO-04 with the correction the installation rule gives it and with `NoCorrection()`; V-BO-03 run past 500 s until its layer's invocation (§12.4 item 6). Per run the table records the invocations, and per invocation the cells below `ε_m` and the cells the band took with the largest merged mass fraction. Pass: one invocation per layer in every run, with the largest merged fraction at most 5 `ε_m` (so the band, fixed before the ladder, cannot be widened to force the count; the prototype's 3.6 % at 60 cells passes, its 10 % at 120 cells fails). The prototype already fails this strict criterion on three rows (A.3, A.4): 120 cells (two invocations, the band saturated at 10 %), `ε_m = 3e-2` (two) and `τ = 1` (two); the 120-cell and `τ_p = 1` rows are the `N²` scaling of §3.2 (a constant layer time `τ` across the ladder is the lever, A.10), the `3e-2` row the absolute band's 3.3 `ε_m` there, which 3 `ε_m` failed at 60 cells and any run left at two invocations is an owner exception recorded per run, not a pass. Every run of the matrix is also judged on all of its case's QoIs (item 2). Measured at M4 with the end-of-step event and the reactivity switch; re-read on the final defaults at M6c (§14.4), the merged-fraction clause watched (R46).
2. **Peak MLR, its timing, and the burst.** `burnout_ladder.jl` runs every V-BO case of the matrix in the suite's assert mode against the cases' own fixed references (V-BO-01: peak 32.39 within 3 %, its time 335.0 s within 3 %, the burnout time 363.5 s within 1 %, the mass loss within 0.3 points, the final thickness, the ledger, the surface-Newton count), and reports the old core at the same resolution in the same session beside each run. The peak and its time are read on the main peak: over the saved times before the solve's last deactivation event (the new core's last invocation, the old core's last park), or over the whole run when there is none (C86). Pass: every run of the matrix passes its case, the 30-cell row included. The burst after the layer's last event is judged by refinement on both cores rather than by a curve against the old core: V-BO-01 at 30, 60, 120 and 240 PMMA cells, `ε_m` of 1e-2 and 3e-3, in both forms, with the gas-generation rate recorded at every accepted step and every post-event state and `t_pk` the old core's 240-cell main-peak time. (a) The largest per-step excess of the N-cell rate over the 2N-cell rate after `t_pk` falls at least 1.5× per grid doubling and is no larger than the old core's at the same N; (b) the L1 of successive grids over [`t_pk`, `t_end`] falls at least 1.5× per doubling, and the new core's L1 against its own 240-cell run is within 1.5× of the old core's at each N; (c) the L1 against the old core's 240-cell run falls with refinement. The same measures are reported, not asserted, on V-BO-03 (extended), V-BO-06 and V-BO-07 and at `ε_m = 3e-2`; a run there whose per-step excess stops falling, or exceeds the old core's, goes to the owner. The decision review's standing: every criterion passes, with and without location (Appendix C.5).
3. **Ledgers closed.** The mass ledger of every V-BO run at its case's tolerance in the temperature form (V-BO-01: 1e-3, the prototype 7e-5 to 2.5e-4; V-BO-02: C39's 3e-3 unless question 7 changes the restart); the energy ledger (`energy_ledger.jl` extended to V-BO-04 at 40 cells, whose event is one and exactly timed, over the event-free window before it and, with located events, across it, the event's vented gas, lost inventory, regularization and created volume entering both closures as the event records them, the sum over live cells only) no larger than the old core's closure on the same deck and converging at the integrator's order under fixed-step refinement on the event-free window; exact in the enthalpy form.
4. **Admissibility at every event.** The deactivation callback asserts it (Phase 1); `burnout_ladder.jl` counts events, invocations and admissibility failures (zero) over the matrix.
5. **Charring decks identical to Phase 1, split by installation.** `burnout_identity.jl`, its reference recorded at M0 on `bb2860b` and re-recorded where §14.6 lists: the hash of `(T, m, x, t)` of V-CPL-02/02b, V-CPL-03, V-CPL-06's char-layer column, V-RX-02d (a residue former), V-HC-05, V-RAD-01 and the Douglas fir deck (60 s, deactivation off). (A) A deck on which the installation rule does not install the correction (a fixed mesh, no deactivation, or no layer that can vanish: V-CPL-03, V-CPL-06a, V-HC-05, V-RAD-01, V-RX-02d and the Douglas fir deck) equals its reference digest; the same deck with `EquidistributionCorrection()` given explicitly agrees to 1e-10 relative in every output on the same accepted-step sequence (a changed sequence is reported with the temperature difference, as Phase 1's 7e-6 K on ale_darcy 25 was), its correction velocity exactly zero and each deck's smallest `h/h₀` recorded (C56). (B) A deck on which the rule installs it (V-CPL-02 and V-CPL-02b; among the routed cases also V-BO-04) passes its QoIs in both forms, its digest at the package defaults is the reference, and the largest activation, the active nodes and the steps against `NoCorrection()` are recorded; V-BO-04's correction is exactly zero, its column being uniform.
6. **Constraint residuals and the domain test.** `burnout_ladder.jl` records per run, on one trajectory (D71), the largest accepted scaled residual under C52's scale and under Phase 1's, and the accepted states Phase 1's scale would have rejected; none is a pass, and the domain test's rejections are reported (the domain test rejects above one, so "at or below one" cannot fail and is not the criterion; the prototype's 0.69 and 52 of 1,337 proposals are the Phase 1-scale numbers). A second scale in the package would be a legacy mode (D99).
7. **Event timing.** `case_accuracy.jl` on V-BO-01, 02, 04 and V-CPL-02/02b with location off and on: the achieved temperature error over the cells live in both solves, and the event-time shift between the tolerance and the tolerance divided by 100 as a fraction of the median event interval. Phase 1's budget (the shift at most ten times `reltol`) is unreachable by three orders of magnitude (A.5: 0.133 off, 0.022 on), so the criterion is restated and the script's budget changed: pass when the located shift is at most 0.05 of the median interval and at least three times smaller than the discrete one on every deactivating case (A.5 passes both on V-BO-02); the default follows (question 5).
8. **Wall time no worse than the old core's burnout runs.** `burnout_timing.jl`, table `measurements/burnout_timing.md`: V-BO-01, 03 and 04 at 30, 60 and 120 cells, both cores in one process, on the final defaults with location on and on a quiet machine, matched on the window RMS before the first event (D58, C70), each core against its own reference at reltol 1e-9, the old default timed over nine solves (D64); and the cost of the correction where the installation rule installs it (V-CPL-02/02b, `ale` and `ale_darcy` at 25 and 100 cells with deactivation on), against the same deck with `NoCorrection()` and against the old core (a deck on which the rule does not install it is bit-identical, item 5, and costs nothing). Pass: the same order as the old core, that is a ratio at most 1.5 on every deck at matched accuracy; 1.5 to 2 is the owner's call; above 2 is the bad sign of §5.7. Standing: M2's acceptance at about 1.5× under `Rodas5P` (§12.4) and M4's tables at a load of 15 to 16, which do not decide the item; the decision review's `FBDF` with an in-process fix of the upstream bug at 0.56× (modeled) and 0.88× (wall) on V-BO-01 (Appendix C.1), to be re-measured on the release. If MF has landed, R40's levers (ii) to (v) leave the phase gate; if not, R40 stands as the owner accepted it at M2.
9. **Smooth finite-difference gradients.** `burnout_gradients.jl` (W3), table `measurements/burnout_gradients.md`: the Phase 0 item 1 protocol on the new core at the package defaults, V-BO-01 with the PMMA absorption coefficient perturbed at 60 and 120 cells and the incident flux and the pre-exponential factor at 60, nine perturbations each (`ε ∈ {0, ±1, ±2, ±4, ±8}·1e-3`), central differences at four steps, the old core in the same process. Phase 0 found the integrated quantity smooth through 60 parks (spread 0.025) and the event-timed ones not (`t_peak` interpolated 0.11, `t_bo` interpolated 0.20); the clause's target is the event-timed quantities. Pass: `peak_mlr` (interpolated), `t_peak` (interpolated), `t_bo` (interpolated) and the invocation time each with a spread below 0.1 across the four steps, and the invocation count the same for every perturbation; a count that changes fails the item, with the design's bracketed differencing as the documented consequence. The safety-net fallbacks (an invocation whose smallest merged fraction is below `ε_m(1 − 1e-4)`) are reported. The decision review passed it on `7ceaf74` with location on (Appendix C.2); the gate's reading is M6c's, under `FBDF` if MF has landed. Smooth event times are a property the correction delivers, not the reason it is the primary burnout path (§10); no current fit consumes these gradients.
10. **Minimum cell thickness.** `burnout_ladder.jl` records the smallest live cell per run; pass when it is at least `0.5 ε_m h₀` on every run (the prototype: 1.03 µm against `ε_m h₀ = 0.98` µm at 60 cells, 2.0/1.97 at 30, 0.49/0.49 at 120, 0.31/0.29 at `ε_m = 3e-3`) and no run ends in `DtLessThanMin`.
11. **The fallback.** If items 1 or 2 fail on V-BO-01 at the defaults after M4's tuning, or item 8 fails and the owner grants no exception, the core ships with `c = 0`, the block-and-band rule and the slot-shifting interior merge (C54): that departs from the design's "nothing else changes", because at `c = 0` the new core cannot finish V-BO-01 or V-BO-03 without an interior policy (A.1). The fallback's own acceptance: V-BO-01 to V-BO-05 pass their cases on the new core at `c = 0` (one invocation per cell on V-BO-01 and 03, as the old core's parks), the ledgers and admissibility of items 3 and 4, item 8's timing with the old core's one-cell events as the comparison. The cutover case is then argued on the exact Jacobian, one-invocation cascades and multi-D readiness alone (design §6).
12. **Phase 1's items 2 and 9 under `FBDF`** (only if MF lands). Phase 1's item 2 (`audit_decks.jl`: the audit decks within 1.5× of the old core at matched accuracy, C40's exception) is re-measured under the new default; within 1.5× closes the exception. Phase 1's item 9 (`finite_slab.jl`: V-HC-02c and V-HC-03 at 320 cells, D37's bound) is read at matched tolerance, consistently with item 8: the default passes when some rung of the ladder (reltol 1e-6 down to 1e-8) meets D37's bound and, at the loosest such rung, its modeled cost is at most 1.5× the old default's at reltol 1e-6. The decision review's `FBDF` meets the bound at 3e-7 (5.961e-4 against 5.992e-4) with 279 right-hand sides and one Jacobian, against `Rodas5P`'s 549 and 61 at 1e-6, and misses it at 1e-6 (6.715e-4), as C28 found.

### 7.4 The decision points at M2 and M4

M2 ends on §7.3 item 8 measured on V-BO-02 and the prototype's V-BO-01 run with the levers of §5.7 applied: within 1.5× the phase continues; above 2× it stops for the owner's decision (continue, ship `c = 0` with the fallback, or reconsider the cutover), and between the two the owner decides.


M4 ends with the matrix of item 1 at 60 and 120 cells. If V-BO-01 ends in one invocation at every threshold at both resolutions with the QoIs of item 2 within tolerance (the prototype's standing: one invocation at 60 cells for `ε_m` of 3e-3 and 1e-2, two at 120 cells and at 3e-2, A.3 and A.4), M5 to M7 proceed as planned. If not, the owner chooses between tuning (`τ`, the band, the activation) within M4's allowance and the fallback of item 11, which adds the slot-shifting interior merge (about one and a half weeks, unverified, C54) before M5. Under the fallback M5 survives unchanged (event location applies to any deactivation), M6's three cases assert the documented per-layer invocation count of item 1's fallback clause instead of one, V-BO-07's `β` measurement is dropped, and the gradient re-measurement runs through the many events as Phase 0 did.

## 8. Risk register

Carried from design §5 and plan §8 (R20 to R22 are Phase 2's) and extended with what this session measured.

| # | Risk | Where it shows | Mitigation / owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| R20 | Event ordering under a parameter perturbation makes finite-difference gradients non-smooth | M6, item 9 | With one invocation per layer there is one event time to differentiate; measured by the Phase 0 protocol; the bracketed differencing of design §5 if it persists |
| R21 | `ε_m` small enough for one event per layer is too small for the Newton | M4 | Measured: 3e-3 gives one invocation and 0.31 µm cells with no stall (A.4); `Rodas5P` has no Newton; the domain test and the step counts are the gate quantities |
| R22 | The correction fails to carry a layer, or needs problem-dependent tuning (Furzeland) | M4, §7.4 | The prototype carries V-BO-01 at `τ = 0.2` with a band of 10 `ε_m` in one invocation at 60 cells for `ε_m` of 3e-3 and 1e-2, in two at 120 cells and at 3e-2 (A.3, A.4); the absolute band is unmeasured at the other thresholds; V-BO-06/07/08 test the tuning; the fallback is item 11 |
| R25 | **An end-of-layer MLR spike.** After the band merge the last per cent of the layer gasifies in a burst: at 30 PMMA cells the peak reads 36.7 against the old core's 32.25 at that resolution (+14 %), and at 60 cells with `τ_p = 0.1` the peak moves to the merge time (A.4); at 60 and 120 cells with `τ_p = 0.2` there is no spike. The mechanism from the rule as written: with first-order kinetics the merge changes the total rate `Σ m_i k(T_i) s_i` only through the switch and the temperature, and it changes both: the merged residue was throttled (`s` 0.74 to 1 inside the band) and the substrate receiver's switch is one, releasing up to 1.35×; the thin film transmits most of the incident flux into a receiver that absorbs it in a fraction of a micrometre, so the merged temperature is above the film's, and 5 K is +27 % in the rate at 650 K; the 30-cell receiver has twice the heat capacity and pulls the merged temperature further toward the substrate's, which is why it spikes more than `τ_p = 0.1` at 60 cells despite the tighter throttling there. The peak QoI is a point sample of a 2 to 3 s burst on a 0.5 s output grid | M4, item 2 | The tail diagnostic of M4: at the last pre-merge step record per cell `m_i`, `T_i`, `s_i`, at the post-event state the residue and `T_m`, decompose `Σ m_i k(T_i) s_i → m_res k(T̄) s̄ → m_res k(T_m)`, compare the burst's integrated mass with the merged residue, and repeat with the switch off (a surviving spike is the temperature part). Merging into the lowest layer cell as the survivor removes only the switch part and trades a second invocation against item 1; the structural options are C51's. The owner decides the switch (question 12) and the 30-cell re-pin (question 8) on that table *Closed by the decision review:* the burst is the merged temperature, not the switch (C76); the old core bursts too and both converge at about first order; item 2 judges it by refinement on both cores and reads the peak on the main peak, and the switch is deleted (§14) |
| R26 | Wall time 3.5 to 6× the old core's at equal tolerance, more at matched accuracy (C49, item 8); the owner's rule is the same order or better | M2, item 8 | The levers of §5.7 in their measured order, the integrator diagnosis first; a stop at M2's gate above 2× (§7.4) |
| R27 | `τ` is a time and the burnout time scale varies by material and flux (Furzeland) | M4, M6 | The `N²` form makes `τ` the layer's equidistribution time, mesh-independent (A.10); the ladder's `τ` row; V-BO-07 and V-BO-08 measure `T_sync` against the layer's life; a `setup` keyword |
| R28 | The band semantics: a multiple of `ε_m` takes cells at 29 % of their mass when `ε_m = 3e-2` (A.4) | M3 | The band is an absolute mass fraction (§4.3) |
| R29 | The correction's stiffness raises the step count and the rejections (1,091 steps at `τ = 0.2`, 1,286 at 0.1, against 853 on the old core) | M4 | `τ` default from the ladder; C52; the restart policy (A.6) |
| R30 | The widened pattern costs 1.7× per Jacobian (30 against 18 colors) | M1 | Measured and accepted; a narrower stencil would be first-order and kinked (C21) and is not pursued |
| R31 | Trial-state gaps on the relative-flux path (C47: a `NaN` face temperature throws) | M1, M3 | C47 fixed; §6.1 item 12 drives the kernel with `NaN`, zero and negative states |
| R32 | A deck's layer interface is not declared, so the correction mixes two materials across it | M3 | The explicit `lagrangian` list is required whenever a correction is selected on a deck with more than one condensed composition; `setup` refuses the combination otherwise |
| R33 | The fallback needs the slot-shifting merge, which is a cross-cutting contract (C54) | §7.4 | Scheduled only if the gate fails; its cost estimated at one and a half weeks |
| R34 | The three new cases have no old-core reference for peak and timing (the old core parks interior cells and may differ at the grading) | M6 | Compared against the old core at the same mesh in the same session; a difference is reported with both values, not pinned silently |
| R35 | The enthalpy form at 3 to 4× the temperature form's time on the burnout decks (A.4) | M4 | The temperature form stays the default (C50); the enthalpy form runs the gate only |
| R36 | The Douglas fir deck at 49 colors is 1.9× the old core at equal tolerance (A.8) | Phase 3 | Re-measured after M2 over the example's 600 s: 1.89× at equal tolerance, 1.42× matched on the RMS, 0.73× at reltol 1e-5 (A.8); the cutover table (question 9) carries it with R40 |
| R37 | A re-pinned QoI hides a regression | M3, M4, M6 | Every re-pin lists the old value, the new value and the reason (C45, C46, R25), and is the owner's |
| R38 | The located event is applied at an interpolated state of the method's order | M5 | The safety net; the admissibility check; item 7 measures the error against the tolerance with location on |
| R39 | Two prototype runs in one session once showed no events where a clean session shows two invocations (A.3's note) | M1 | The runs were discarded; the package tests of §6.1 item 16 (repeated solves bit-identical with events) guard the package implementation |
| R40 | The burnout path stays at about 1.5× the old core at matched accuracy (V-BO-01) because `Rodas5P` builds an exact Jacobian every step | Phase gate | The owner accepted M2 at about 1.5×. Lever (i), a reusing integrator, is `FBDF` once the upstream fix is released (MF, §14): with an in-process fix it ran V-BO-01 at 0.56× modeled and 0.88× wall (Appendix C.1), and if item 8 passes under it the levers (ii) to (v) leave the phase gate. Without a release, R40 stands as accepted |
| R41 | The `N²` gain on a long single-material layer with deactivation on: `ale_darcy` at 100 cells takes 1,623 steps against 470 for the band alone (`Rodas5P`) | W5, MF, item 8 | Measured at W5 and under `FBDF`. If it persists, the lever is the gain on long layers, which costs V-BO-01 its single invocation if weakened uniformly; the owner's decision |
| R42 | No OrdinaryDiffEqBDF release contains the upstream fix by M6b's end | MF | Question 17: M6c waits at most a week, then Phase 2 closes on `Rodas5P` and MF follows the release |
| R43 | The released fix chooses `dt` and the order after a domain rejection differently from the review's in-process fix, and `FBDF`'s advantage shrinks | MF, item 8 | W6's pre-release measurement on #4662's merge commit, and on #4695's if it merges. *Measured for #4662* (Appendix C.7): `FBDF` keeps about half `Rodas5P`'s cost on every deck; #4695 remains to check if a release carries it |
| R44 | `FBDF`'s error is not monotone in the tolerance, so an unmeasured deck can show a gap: V-BO-03, the 120- and 240-cell rungs, the enthalpy form, or item 9's smoothness under a variable-order method | MF, M6c | MF's runs before the default is committed; a deck that fails stops the milestone for the owner |

## 9. Branch and process

### 9.1 Branches and the `main` rule

`mesh-framework-phase2` is branched off `mesh-framework-phase1` at `bb2860b` and returns to it by pull request; this plan opens the draft pull request. **Nothing goes to `main` until the owner commits to this architecture at the cutover decision:** no push to `main`, no change to pull request #12 (`mesh-framework-phase1` → `main`), which the owner alone handles. This supersedes plan §9.1 and C40's "the draft pull request becomes a real one": #12 stays as the owner left it, and Phase 2's work is reviewed against `mesh-framework-phase1`. Every commit is GPG-signed, one theme per commit, the subject imperative, the body stating what, why and the evidence; no AI attribution of any kind anywhere (commits, pull requests, issues, files); the draft pull request's body is this plan's summary (§11) and its owner questions (§13). The Quality workflow (`.github/workflows/quality.yml`) triggers only on `main`, so it never runs on the draft pull request: the format check (`julia --project=scripts scripts/format.jl --check`), Aqua and JET are run locally before every push, as plan §9.3 does, and the workflow can be run by hand on the branch (`workflow_dispatch`).

### 9.2 The prototype as evidence

`scripts/mesh_phase2_prototype/` holds five files: `common.jl` (the method overrides, in a dedicated Julia session only, never in a session that runs the package's tests), `run.jl` (the prototype's experiments by name: `check`, `sweep`, `band`, `repeat`, `ladder`, `order`, `continuous`, `scaling`), `baselines.jl` (the measurements that override nothing: `vbo_old`, `vbo_new`, `vbo_ladder_old`, `vbo01_qois_old`, `vbo02_restart`, `df_cores`, `integrators`, `tolweights`), `cases.jl` (the case runner the two `vbo_*` baselines use) and `README.md` (what is overridden, how to run, that it is planning evidence and not package code). Every script prints its table to standard output; no measurement file is written, and the tables are Appendix A, so the "scripts and tables" commit holds the scripts alone. The directory is formatted with the repository's formatter and committed in its own commit before the plan. `common.jl` and `run.jl` are deleted when M3 lands, since M3 reproduces the one-invocation run inside the package and M2 measures on the prototype's run (§5.7, §7); `baselines.jl` and `cases.jl` stay until their measurements have gate scripts of their own (§6.3). *Owner (R-1, at the review's followup):* the directory is deleted. `vbo_old`, `vbo_new`, `vbo_ladder_old`, `vbo01_qois_old` and `df_cores` have gate scripts (`burnout_ladder.jl`, `burnout_identity.jl`, `burnout_timing.jl`), `vbo02_restart` is D80's measurement and a test, and `integrators` and `tolweights` stay as A.7 and A.9 record them, reproducible from `8166e77`.

### 9.3 Commit and review discipline

As plan §9.3: one theme per commit; replaced rules deleted; no dates or historical notes in code or docstrings; the guides untouched; `julia --project=scripts scripts/format.jl` before every commit (the formatter covers `scripts/`); Aqua and JET after any change to exports, dependencies or signatures; targeted gates per milestone and the full three-way `Pkg.test()` at M7; the old-core fingerprint and the old core's `Pkg.test()` after any edit of Materials or BoundaryConditions; gate tables stamped with their commit and its tree, and measured in a detached worktree of a signed commit, never of a commit still to be signed or rebased (D98). The development loop of Phase 1 (a scratch environment mirroring the root manifest, Revise job servers, never two processes precompiling Pyrolysis at once, measurements inside functions, both cores in one process, timings repeated; recorded in the owner's local Phase 1 handoff) applies unchanged, with one addition from this planning: a server in which a job has overridden package methods is retired before any package test runs in it, and a job that fails while defining an override leaves the server unusable (R39).

## 11. Summary

**What Phase 2 builds.** The correction `c` of design §3.3 on the new core: a per-node relaxed equidistribution of a monitor with a smooth per-node activation in the cells' thickness ratio, carried by the balance rows as a sweep term so that `w − c` is the solid velocity, with the existing relative-flux kernel reading `−c` as the relative velocity and every inventory, the enthalpy and the history pair crossing faces with the same fluxes; the Jacobian pattern widened for it and verified by containment; the cascade rule that the design's one-callback-per-step needs, a block of near-threshold cells from the exposed side merging into the cell beneath in one invocation; event location by a continuous callback with the discrete predicate as its safety net; the constraint scale, the outputs, the surface Newton's trial clamp and a trial-state gap closed. The old core is untouched except for two additive, fingerprint-checked edits (the clamp keyword, an optional material field). V-BO-01, 03, 04 and 05 join the new core with re-pins the owner approves; three burnout cases are added (graded, heterogeneous, localized).

**What the evidence says.** A scratch prototype of exactly this design on the Phase 1 code carries the V-BO-01 PMMA layer through burnout in one deactivation at 60 PMMA cells for `ε_m` of 1e-2 and 3e-3 with every quantity of interest within its tolerance (peak within 0.01 %, burnout time exact, ledger 7e-5), in two invocations at 120 cells and at `ε_m = 3e-2`, with cells shrinking to a micrometre and no stall; the pieces that made it work and were not in the design's text are the per-node "or" activation and the near-threshold band, both recorded as conflicts. The cost is 3.5 to 6× the old core's at equal tolerance, mostly the per-step Jacobian at 30 colors, and more at matched accuracy; no integrator candidate closes Phase 1's accuracy-per-tolerance gap; event location ties the event times to the tolerance for 2 to 4 % of the solve; a forced restart buys the old ledger tolerance back for 30 %. At 30 cells the end of the layer shows an MLR spike that the ladder must settle.

**What it costs.** Eleven and a half to fourteen and a half weeks of one developer, the review and a performance milestone included (the design said 4 to 6); thirteen to sixteen if the correction fails its gate and the slot-shifting merge is built; seventeen new tests, three new cases, four new gate scripts and two extended; no change to the old core beyond the clamp keyword (default unchanged) and an optional material field.

**What its gate proves.** One invocation per layer across grids, thresholds and monitor settings, with a graded grid, a heterogeneous layer and a localized layer among the checks; the peak and its timing against the old core; closed ledgers; admissibility at every event; the charring decks and the Douglas fir deck bit-identical without a correction; constraint residuals and domain rejections; event times tied to the tolerance; wall time at matched accuracy against the old core, reported with its spread; smooth event-timed finite differences through the one event, a property the correction delivers rather than its reason (§10); the minimum cell size.

**What remains open after it.** The burnout path's last factor of about 1.5× against the old core, which M2 left with the owner's acceptance and which `FBDF` closes on the decks measured once the upstream fix of its domain-rejection bug is released (R40, MF in §14); without the release the levers of §12.4 stay carried; the interior merge on a moving mesh at `c = 0` (built only as the fallback); the design's items for later phases (radial Beer–Lambert, P1, the variable cross section's fate, multi-D deactivation by element death); the cutover's end-to-end comparison on the real wood decks, for which the Douglas fir deck now runs on the new core.

---

## 12. Implementation status

Branch `mesh-framework-phase2`, draft pull request #14 against `mesh-framework-phase1`. M0 to M7 are done, M4 closed on the owner's decisions of §12.4 and M6 split into M6a to M6c by the decision review's implementation (§14); the gate is `docs/Mesh_Framework_Phase2_Gate.md`, measured on `Rodas5P`, and MF waits for the upstream release. The Phase 2 review (`docs/Mesh_Framework_Phase2_Review.md`) and its followup (`docs/Mesh_Framework_Phase2_Review_Followup_Plan.md`, branch `phase2-review`) fixed its findings and corrected the gate (D90 to D101, C95 to C97). Departures continue the series at D53, conflicts at C57; this section records each milestone's commits and gate evidence as plan §12.1 did.

### 12.2 Departures from this plan

- **D53. One merge per run.** The block-and-band rule of §3.4 sums the run's inventories into the receiver once and inverts the merged temperature once, the pore rule applied to the run and the receiver together (`_merge!`, which replaces `_transfer!` and `_merge_volume!`). A one-cell merge performs exactly the Phase 1 transfer's arithmetic, so the fixed-mesh rule, which still merges the outermost cell while it is below `ε_m` and re-tests the receiver (D47), goes through the same function and stays bit-identical. The events stay one per dead cell (`park_times` one per cell, C53), outermost first, all naming the run's receiver; the merge's created volume, losses and regularization are written to its first event and the others carry zeros, so the sums over the events remain the ledger's terms (D48). Every event carries its invocation (numbered from one over the solve) and the dead cell's condensed mass as a fraction of its reference when it went, from which item 1's per-invocation counts follow (cells below `ε_m`, cells the band took, the largest merged fraction). A run that reaches the innermost cell leaves it as the survivor; a survivor still below `ε_m` is the last live cell and goes in the same invocation (`:burned_out`).
- **D54. The band is a keyword from M1, and defaults to the threshold (C58).** `setup` and `solve` take `band` now, since the rule reads it, checked to lie in `[threshold, 1)` when cells are deactivated. Its default is the threshold itself, so without a correction the moving-mesh rule is the contiguous block of cells below `ε_m` at the exposed side; the default of 0.1 in §3.9 goes with the equidistribution correction at M3.
- **D55. The surface Newton is handed its trial clamp (C36).** `solve_boundary_temperature!` takes `trial`, a smooth clamp `T -> T_c`; its default `nothing` keeps the hard clamp of the iterate and both warnings, which the old core uses unchanged. The new core's thermal boundary kernel passes `trial_temperature`: the condition and its blowing face are evaluated at `trial(T_s)`, the Newton derivative carries the clamp's slope at the iterate's value (the dual fixed point is the implicit-function derivative whatever that slope's own partials, as for the frozen blowing derivative), the iterate is never clamped and neither warning is issued. The balance then has a root at any cell temperature: beyond the bracket the condition is constant and the face follows the cell one for one. The clamp is passed rather than named because `BoundaryConditions` loads before `States`. On a physical state the trial temperature is the identity and the result is bit-identical (asserted at six states with and without outflow, values and derivatives).
- **D56. C47 returns `T * NaN`.** `_tabulated` and `enthalpy_slope` return `T * NaN` at a `NaN` temperature, value and partials `NaN`; infinite temperatures already took the linear branches.
- **D57. The material's residual-thickness fraction moves to M3 (question 4).** Nothing reads it before the activation thresholds exist; M3 adds it beside its reader and runs the fingerprint and the old core's `Pkg.test()` then.
- **D58. Matched accuracy on the burnout decks is the window before the first event.** §7.3 item 8 measures a deck with events by its temperature error over the saved times and live cells plus the event-time shift. On V-BO-02 that error does not fall with the tolerance on either core (the old core 50.3, 30.4 and 39.7 K at reltol 1e-5, 1e-6 and 1e-7, `Rodas5P` 17 to 29 K; the old core's reference at 1e-9 differs from one at 1e-10 by 10.6 K and its event times by 0.07 of the median interval): a cell deactivates at the end of the step on which it crosses the threshold, during a slow approach on which the step is long whatever the tolerance, and the temperatures after an event follow its time. `burnout_timing.jl` therefore matches on the largest temperature difference over the cells at the saved times before the first event of any solve of the deck (62.4 s on V-BO-02, 167.8 s on V-BO-01), each core against its own reference at reltol 1e-9 (spreads against 1e-10: 0.031 and 3.3e-5 K on V-BO-02, 0.016 and 7.0e-5 K on V-BO-01), takes a configuration's loosest ladder tolerance at or below the old default's error, and reports the whole-run error, the event shift, the mass loss rate's L1 error and the case's QoIs beside it. The item is re-measured on the whole run once M5 locates the events.
- **D59. The prototype runs on the branch head and evaluates its correction once per residual.** M1 deleted `_transfer!`, which the prototype's deactivation override called: the override is removed, the package's block-and-band rule takes its place, and `build_vbo` passes the band as `κ ε_m`. The relative-flux kernel now evaluates the node corrections once per residual evaluation, each cell's activation once, and skips a face whose relative velocity is exactly zero, as §3.1's `ds.c` will; the velocity rows read the stored values. The trajectory is bit-identical (V-BO-01 at `τ` 0.2 and `κ` 10: digest `bffba3a46bd1441c` before and after, 1,090 steps, 245 rejections, 11,770 right-hand sides), the residual at 300 s is 31.8 µs instead of 59.9 µs, and the `check` experiment passes (no missing entries, colored equal to dense). Appendix A's tables were taken on `bb2860b` with the prototype as it was.
- **D60. The gas-transport kernel evaluates the gas components' enthalpies only.** Its face energy flux carries the gas mass fluxes, so the solid enthalpies it evaluated at the face temperature were multiplied by zero; `component_enthalpies(laws, T, flags)` returns zero for the components not flagged and the kernel passes its gas flags. Bit-identical: the residual and the colored Jacobian are unchanged at three states (initial, 200 s, a perturbed state with negative entries) on six decks (V-BO-01 in both forms, V-BO-02, the `ale_darcy` and `eulerian` audit decks at 25 cells, the Douglas fir deck at 20 s); the residual at 200 s falls from 24.7 to 21.7 µs on V-BO-01 and from 19.9 to 17.9 on V-BO-02. Evaluating the matrix volume fractions once per cell for the conductivity, emissivity and absorptivity (three calls of `mixing_volume_fractions` into one) measured within the run-to-run spread (22.3 to 24.1 µs before, 23.4 to 24.0 after, alternating in one server) and is not kept. What remains of the right-hand side's 1.4× over the old core's at 200 s is `derive!` (11 of 22 µs: the heat capacities' power laws and the tabulated enthalpies, which the old core pays too, plus the positive parts, the floors and the per-cell enthalpies it does not) and the lifted rows' kernels (Beer–Lambert 1.7, closure 1.1, assembly 1.3 µs).
- **D61. The heuristic restart after an event is the default (§12.4, decision 2; C65).** `IntegratorPolicy` takes a third restart kind, `:heuristic`: after an event the integrator's initial-step heuristic (`auto_dt_reset!`, two right-hand sides) proposes the step. `rodas5p_policy` defaults to it, and so does `default_policy`. Under it the domain-test fixture of `test_deactivation.jl` (an eight-cell radiating ablator at reltol 1e-10) passes its four events without a domain rejection, so that test asks for the solver-chosen restart to reach the rejection and give-up paths. On the identity decks with events, V-CPL-02 and V-CPL-02b take 2,049 and 1,192 steps with 2 rejections each, against 1,904 and 1,057 steps with 741 and 357, and their temperatures move by at most 0.015 and 0.165 K, through the event times (D58).
- **D62. The sparse factorization learns the union of `W`'s nonzeros, on every layout (§12.4, decision 3a; C68).** `StructuredKLU` keeps the union of the prototype's entries that have been nonzero in the solve, with the diagonal, copies each fresh `W` into a matrix of that structure and refactors KLU numerically while the union holds; a nonzero outside it grows the union, and KLU analyses the grown structure afresh. `default_linear_solver()` returns it on every layout and takes no argument. Tests: a unit test (the first `W` grows the union by its nonzeros and keeps a stored zero out, a `W` within the union refactors without growing it, a new nonzero grows it to the prototype, an entry outside the prototype is an error) and, on a moving and a fixed mesh, at most one analysis per growth, a union smaller than the prototype that holds the diagonal, and steps and rejections equal to plain KLU's with temperatures within 1e-9 K. The identity decks without events move by round-off (at most 1.1e-11 K, the same steps). With D61 six of the eight digests of §7.3 item 5 change, and the identity reference is re-recorded on this tree.
- **D63. The right-hand side skips absent components (§12.4, decision 3b).** Profiled at V-BO-02's 200 s state, a third of `derive!` was the deck's PMMA power laws `c(T)` and `k(T)` evaluated in the 122 Kaowool cells, where the PMMA mass is exactly zero. `derive!` sums the heat capacity without evaluating the law of a component whose mass is exactly zero (`heat_capacity_sum`); the conductivity takes an opt-in `skip_absent`, which the new core passes; the reaction kernel skips a cell in which every reaction lacks a reactant (under the one-sided limiter its rates and their derivatives are then exactly zero). `iszero` of a dual number reads its partials, so the Jacobian keeps every entry. The conductivity's skip is opt-in because `Properties` is shared: the old core's call is unchanged, its fingerprint equals 17bc477's, and its cost, the gate's denominator, does not move. Bit-identical: the residual and the colored Jacobian at three states on the six decks of D60. The residual at 200 s: V-BO-01 27.4 → 22.4 µs (old core 15.5; `derive!` 11.3 → 8.7, the reaction kernel 2.8 → 1.4), V-BO-02 19.7 → 14.8 µs (old core 13.1).
- **D64. `burnout_timing.jl` matches on the window RMS (§12.4; C70).** The old default is timed over nine solves, the new core runs its default integrator only, and the table no longer reports a KLU refactorization timed on a freshly analysed `W` (C68): the factorization's cost is read from the profiler's shares, which count KLU's analysis, its pivot-growth check and the union's bookkeeping as factorization.
- **D65. The correction's parameters ride on its object, and `setup` binds it to the mesh.** `EquidistributionCorrection(; τ, activation, monitor)` holds `τ`, `(r_hi, r_lo)` and `(α, β)`, so the activation is a keyword of the correction rather than of `setup` (§2.2, §4.5 list it beside `correction`). `setup` and `solve` take `correction` (default `NoCorrection()`) and `lagrangian` (the layer interfaces, passed to `line_mesh`), and `setup` binds the correction to the mesh (`bind_correction`) as a `LayerCorrection`: per node the gain `N²/τ` of its layer, zero at the layers' bounding nodes (the pinned node, the Lagrangian nodes and the end nodes), per cell the reciprocal of its layer's mean initial monitor (C72). `N` is the layer's cell count at setup, so `τ/N²` is the prototype's per-cell time throughout a run and a merge does not speed up the relaxation of the cells left. `w_override` stays the keyword of the prescribed velocity and becomes a `PrescribedCorrection`, which replaces `BalanceRowClosure.override` (its `correction` field; the balance rows, the consistent sweep, the rank check, the pattern and the domain test dispatch on it). `setup` refuses a correction on a fixed mesh, a correction beside `w_override`, and an equidistribution correction on a deck with an interior node between cells that start with different condensed components which is not a Lagrangian node (R32). Under the equidistribution correction the band defaults to `max(threshold, CORRECTION_BAND)`, `CORRECTION_BAND = 0.1` (D54). `switch_width` (default `SWITCH_WIDTH`, zero for no switch) is the reactivity switch's width, for C51 and question 12. `τ` defaults to `EQUIDISTRIBUTION_TIME = 720` s, the prototype's one-invocation run (`τ_p = 0.2` s at 60 cells) in the `N²` form, until M4's ladder sets it.
- **D66. Before a cell thins below `r_hi` the correction adds exact zeros.** The activation is zero at and above `r_hi h₀` without evaluating its logarithm (the smoothstep's value there), a node whose activation is zero gets `c = 0` with zero partials, and the relative-flux kernel skips a face whose relative velocity is exactly zero (the prototype's skip, D59). Until the first activation the residual is therefore the `NoCorrection` residual; only the pattern and its factorization differ (C71's resolution). The skip applies under a prescribed velocity too (the end nodes of the prescribed decks, where `w = 0`), and changes no digest there.
- **D67. Under the equidistribution correction the cell rows do not read the node velocities.** The relative-flux kernel reads `ds.c`, which reads positions and masses there; only under a prescribed velocity is `c` the node velocity, so the cell rows' two velocity entries are kept for `PrescribedCorrection` only. The count is `5NC + 11 + 2·bl + 11·χ`, two below the formula of design §3.7 that C43 and C66 measured on the prototype (which kept the entries): 28 on V-BO-01 against its 30, the coloring optimal (the largest row) at 6 to 25 cells, and the same with and without the monitor's mass term. The balance rows' reach of §3.6 (the positions of nodes `k − 1 … k + 2`, the masses of cells `k − 1 … k + 1` only with the mass term, no energy) lies inside the cell rows' and adds no color.
- **D68. The outputs.** `CoreSolution.correction_velocity` (nodes × saved times, zero without a correction, `0 × 0` on a fixed mesh, `NaN` beyond the outermost live node), `extras.min_cell_thickness` (the thinnest live cell at each saved time), `SolverStatistics.invocations` (the deactivation invocations, zero without events), and a dead cell's concentrations `NaN` (its masses stay zero). The smallest cell between saved times, which §7.3 item 10 bounds, is not a statistic: M4's `burnout_ladder.jl` records it at every step, as the prototype's recorder did.
- **D69. The material's residual-thickness fraction is not added (D57, question 4).** Its reader would be the activation's default thresholds, and the rule from a residual fraction `f` to `(r_hi, r_lo)` is in neither this plan nor the answers. Proposed: `r_hi = min(0.3, f/2)`, `r_lo = r_hi/3`, so that a layer which keeps `f` of its thickness never activates. With the thresholds a keyword of the correction (D65) a deck can set them now, and no old-core code changes in M3. *Owner:* the field is dropped (§14): keeping the correction off cells that cannot vanish is the installation rule's job (C87), and the rule proposed here gives `r_hi = 0` on a non-charring layer, which turns the correction off where it is needed (Appendix C.3).
- **D70. The prototype runs on the closure's correction field until M4.** Its velocity-row and consistent-sweep overrides dispatch on `NoCorrection` and its pattern override reads `cl.correction` (three signatures; nothing else changed). §9.2 deletes `common.jl` and `run.jl` when M3 lands, but `burnout_timing.jl` still includes `common.jl`, and M4 switches it to the package's correction, so both files go at M4.
- **D71. Item 6 is measured on one trajectory.** §7.3 item 6 asks for the domain rejections before and after C52 on the same commit; C52's scale is unconditional, and a second scale in the package would be a legacy mode. `burnout_ladder.jl` evaluates the residual at every accepted state, before the deactivation event of its step, and records the largest scaled residual under C52's scale (checked against `Integration.constraint_residual` at every state: equal to the last bit) and under Phase 1's, which omits the sweep of the node corrections, with the number of accepted states Phase 1's scale would have rejected; the domain test's rejections are C52's. An accepted state above one under Phase 1's scale is a step C52 kept that Phase 1 would have refused; none is one, so C52 removed no rejection on any run, as C67 found on the prototype.
- **D72. The surface QoI counts accepted states.** The re-pin of question 8 asks for "a count of clamped trial iterates". On a trial state the clamp is D55's purpose and a count of it would count Rosenbrock stages; the case's recorder instead evaluates the residual at every accepted state and counts the live boundary faces whose surface balance did not converge or ended where the trial clamp departs from the identity. No package counter is added. The QoI replaces the warning count on V-BO-02 as well, where it was also trivially zero on the new core.
- **D73. The ladder reads each step's state before its event, and carries R25's diagnostic.** The thinnest cell of item 10 is that of the state that ends a step, before the deactivation event merges it away: a continuous callback whose condition never crosses zero reads it, since OrdinaryDiffEq evaluates continuous conditions before any discrete callback. The same reader keeps the state, so R25's decomposition is a section of `burnout_ladder.jl` for every V-BO-01 run rather than a separate script, and the switch-off runs (`switch_width = 0` at 30, 60 and 120 cells) are rows of the matrix with their QoIs. Item 2's curve criterion is reported with the time of the largest difference and, for scale, the old core against itself at twice the cells.
- **D74. `burnout_timing.jl` on the package's correction.** V-BO-01, 03 and 04 at 30, 60 and 120 cells, each case's tolerance its old default (reltol 1e-7 on V-BO-04); V-BO-04 stays at its initial temperature, so it is matched on the window RMS of its thickness relative to the reference's; the cost anatomy on V-BO-01 at 60 cells only. The inactive correction: the moving audit decks and V-CPL-02/03 without a correction and with an `EquidistributionCorrection()` installed, at equal tolerance, the old core beside. The prototype's `common.jl` and `run.jl` are deleted (D70).
- **D75. V-BO-04's energy ledger is closed over each core's own enthalpy change.** The deck is adiabatic, so the integrated boundary heat that normalizes V-CPL-06's closures is zero; the old core's budget is V-CPL-06b's (its ledger solve takes the gas index and the heat capacities), over 0 to 290 s without deactivation. The fixed-step refinement uses steps of 0.1, 0.05 and 0.025 s: at 0.2 s and above the gas venting at the start of the burn drives the gas masses near the top negative, which a fixed step cannot reject, and the solve diverges.
- **D76. A located event widens the threshold by the relative tolerance (C81).** §3.5 passes the located cell to `deactivate!`, non-strict at the root. `deactivate!` takes a `slack` instead, which widens its threshold and band tests to `1 + slack` times `ε_m` and `ε_band`; the end-of-step event passes zero (the strict test, bit-identical), a located event the solve's `reltol`. At the root the crossing cell holds exactly its threshold and goes, and with it every cell within the relative tolerance of its own: the mass rows' absolute tolerance is `reltol · ε_m · mˢᵢ(0)`, so these are the cells whose crossing the solve does not resolve from the located one. The margin is `(m − ε_m m_ref)/(ε_m m_ref)` (`threshold_margin`) rather than `m/(ε_m m_ref) − 1`, so that its sign is exactly the threshold test's; the callback needs no crossing cell.
- **D77. Item 7 measures invocation times, and a single invocation against the saved times.** V-BO-01 and V-BO-04 deactivate in one invocation of 60 and 39 events at one time, so the median interval between events is undefined. `case_accuracy.jl` compares the invocations' times, and a case with a single invocation divides its shift by the median interval between the reference's saved times (0.5 s on V-BO-01, 2 s on V-BO-04), where its quantities are read; the end-of-step shift is then 0.22 and 0.41 of it on V-BO-01 (temperature and enthalpy forms) and 0.13 and 0.16 on V-BO-04, the located shift 6.0e-3 and 5.8e-6, and 1.2e-5 and 5.2e-6. The criterion is otherwise §7.3 item 7's, and the Phase 1 budget of ten times reltol is gone from the script. *Owner:* kept (§14.8, question 22).
- **D78. Location by default, and V-BO-04's event QoI re-pinned.** Item 7 passes on every deactivating case, so by question 5 `located_events` defaults to true; `located_events = false` leaves the end-of-step event alone. Decks without deactivation build no event callback and do not move. V-BO-04's QoI on the event's delay behind the exact crossing (C57) was 1e-2, observed 6.0e-3, the event following the step that crossed; the located event falls on the crossing to 5.2e-8 (temperature form) and 2.3e-8 (enthalpy form) of its time, so the QoI becomes the event located at the crossing, at 1e-6. The new digests (temperature form, then enthalpy): V-BO-01 `7ddba4f8a126a5da` and `6d52c6ea828d4542`, V-BO-02 `c34a74b6f1ac867e` and `bc673700292dd5ab`, V-BO-04 `44b093ae593006c1` and `a988f495364702ee`, V-CPL-02 `b40f4e3e962f0964` and `2aefdccc711e5d68`, V-CPL-02b `49f04b6bd696c46d` and `5fa83bbb82e204e0`.
