The ladder and timing tables of M4 were measured with the end-of-step event; M6 re-measures them.
- **D79. V-BO-04's ledger across its event.** The inventories are summed over the live cells (the extended energy, the inventory itself in the enthalpy form, and the mass; a dead cell's temperature is NaN), the boundary terms are taken at the live boundary faces (`live_face`), each invocation's lost enthalpy, regularization and lost masses leave the inventories at the event, and the created volume, which enters neither closure, is reported. The trapezoid needs the integrand just before the event, which the step that ends there cannot give (C83), so a continuous probe located where the smallest margin is 1e-8 adds that node. The old core's budget does not park cells, so its closure before its first park, 7.1e-5, is the comparison of item 3.
- **D80. The heuristic restart is kept (question 7).** On V-BO-01 at 60 cells with the correction (the case's tolerances), with location off and on: the solver-chosen restart 1,118 and 1,119 accepted steps with 244 rejections, the heuristic and the forced `DT_AFTER_EVENT` restarts 1,126 and 1,127 with 225 and 224, every QoI passing for all six, the mass ledger 8.1e-7 off and 7.2e-5 on for all three (the event falls at 358.71 s or 358.40 s between the 0.5 s saved times the ledger's trapezoid reads); in the enthalpy form 1,581 to 1,589 steps, ledger 4.6e-5 and 7.3e-5. The layer goes in one invocation and only the Kaowool remains after it, so the restart moves nothing a QoI reads, and D61's default stands. Scratch measurement; wall times at a load of 15 to 20 do not separate the policies.
- **D81. The burst criteria are a gate script of their own.** §14 put the per-step recorder in V-BO-01's case and the criteria in `burnout_ladder.jl`. They are `test/newcore/gates/burnout_burst.jl`, with the recorder in the script, so the suite's solves do not pay for it.
  - **The window** starts at the old core's 240-cell peak QoI time (336.0 s, by C86's definition) rather than at its per-step main peak. Per step, a park before the old core's last park can rise above the main peak: 32.65 at 359.2 s at 30 cells.
  - **Validation:** on `768944e` (the package code of `7ceaf74`, location on) the port reproduced the decision review's located tables to their printed digits with the review's own windows.
- **D82. V-BO-04's thickness tolerance is the core's.** 1e-6 on the finite-volume core (observed 1.5e-9), the old core's 1e-4 unchanged, since only the finite-volume core's law was re-pinned (§14.6, row 1).
- **D83. The equidistribution correction takes `layers`.** §14 (W5) gave a layer that cannot vanish zero gain under any equidistribution correction. That emptied the testsets that exercise the correction's own transport on inert layers: the bounding zeros, the activation, the relaxation, the solid velocity, isothermal advection and containment.
  - **`layers = :vanishing`**, the default and what the rule installs, moves only the layers that can vanish.
  - **`layers = :all`** moves every layer, and those six testsets pass it.
- **D84. `burnout_identity.jl` splits by the installation rule** (§7.3, item 5).
  - **Class:** each deck's is read by setting it up with its mesh motion and deactivation and looking at the installed correction.
  - **Class A on a moving mesh** is solved again with an explicit `EquidistributionCorrection()`. It must take the same accepted and rejected steps, stay within 1e-10 of the first solve (relative to each field's largest value), and keep the correction velocity exactly zero.
  - **Class B** is solved again with `NoCorrection()`; its steps there and the largest correction velocity are recorded.
- **D85. The ladder lets `setup` take the layer interfaces.** `burnout_ladder.jl` passed `lagrangian = [_vbo_interface_node(problem)]`, which assumed a uniform pitch, and the M6b handoff proposed generalizing it for the graded deck. Since W5 `setup` makes the composition interfaces the Lagrangian nodes whenever an equidistribution correction is given without `lagrangian` (C85). That finds the PMMA–Kaowool node on every V-BO deck, the graded and mixed ones included, and is the same node on the uniform ones (none on V-BO-04). The ladder passes no `lagrangian`, and `_vbo_interface_node`, which had no other reader, is deleted.
- **D86. V-BO-08 runs to 150 s and saves every 0.1 s.** Its layer deactivates at 69.08 s in both forms (the old core's last park at 69.55 s), so §6.2's 500 s was mostly substrate heating. At V-BO-01's 0.5 s saves the mass ledger reads 1.08e-3 in both forms, against the case's 1e-3 (the old core 3.2e-4): the residue of the one invocation (the largest merged fraction 4.5 %) gasifies within a second, between saved times. The saves do not move the solve (the same invocation time to the last digit), and the ledger falls to 1.5e-4 at 0.1 s and 2.8e-5 at 0.02 s (the old core 4.6e-5 at 0.1 s), so it is the trapezoid's error. `_vbo_problem` and `_vbo_deck` take `save_every`, 0.5 s by default.
- **D87. V-BO-07's references are the old core's at 240 PMMA cells.** §6.2 left the peak and its timing to the CI run. The old core gives the peak 30.41, 30.54, 30.61 and 30.64 g m⁻² s⁻¹ at 286.0, 287.5, 288.0 and 288.0 s, and burnout at 325.5, 325.0, 324.5 and 325.0 s, at 30, 60, 120 and 240 cells. The references are 30.64 g m⁻² s⁻¹, 288.0 s and 325.0 s at V-BO-01's tolerances (3 %, 3 % and 1 %); the finite-volume core at 60 cells reads 30.542 at 287.5 s and 324.0 s in both forms.
- **D88. V-BO-03's gate rows run to 3,100 s** (`VBO03_T_EXTENDED`, beside the case), past its layer's invocation at about 3,023 s (C75); the case still ends at 500 s and asserts what it pinned. `burnout_ladder.jl` and `burnout_burst.jl` run the extended deck on both cores.
- **D89. V-BO-06 keeps its overall grading across resolutions.** §6.2 gave a ratio of 1.02 per cell across the 60 PMMA cells. Held per cell at every resolution, the cell ladder changed the mesh's shape (the surface cell a third of the interface cell at 60 cells, 0.56 at 30, 0.095 at 120), so it refined nothing. The ratio per cell is `1.02^(59/(n − 1))`, exactly 1.02 at the CI resolution, so the case solves the same deck.
- **D90. The relative-flux stencil reads no dead cell (K-1, TS-1).** `_stencil` took `X = far[side, f]`, the cell beyond the upwind cell, whatever its state; after a merge into receiver `r`, with cell `r` thicker than cell `r − 1` and below the activation bound, the face at node `r` took its slope from the dead cell `r + 1` (its zeroed masses, its frozen temperature, a distance built on its frozen node). It now takes the live-cell mask, and a dead `X` is absent: the face takes the stencil of the outermost cell of a mesh that never had the dead cells, the downwind gradient alone, which is C73's slope (§12.4), so the owner's answer to C73 covers it too. The test (`test_correction.jl`, both forms) merges cell 6 of a six-cell ablator into cell 5 and asserts that cell 6's rows and node 7's position move no live residual row and that no live Jacobian row reads a dead column; on `8166e77` three of the four perturbations and the dead-column test fail in each form. The twelve digests of V-BO-01, 03, 04, 06, 07 and 08 as the suite solves them, both forms, are equal on the parent and the fix (temperature form `747add3a5583f983`, `e127bb9f879e70e`, `4e3cf8a83e9e9078`, `18c88c7fb2ec6929`, `7924f233ef0e1cbc`, `6968282570fb06d0`; enthalpy form `a3ffae9f6184d2c8`, `ca8e96ec36a0058e`, `790896f80424af15`, `30eccaeb09e8154e`, `43ab1cce5671e0f4`, `df43c469bdec869d`). V-CPL-02 and V-CPL-02b are bit-identical too (C97): their digests stay `1003e312050aa0d6` and `5307656821b6050d` (temperature form) and `fb9816e51407f7d5` and `e0b6a9bc5bd17504` (enthalpy form), and their QoIs are unchanged (the energy balance 1.865e-3 and 1.855e-3 of 2.1e-3 on V-CPL-02, 1.798e-3 in both forms of 2.0e-3 on V-CPL-02b; the consistency 2.93e-4 of 2.1e-3 and 3.25e-4 of 1.7e-3 in the temperature form). `ale_darcy` at 25 and 100 cells with deactivation reach the path and change: 585 to 583 and 1,620 to 1,623 accepted steps.
- **D91. The band's default follows the bound correction (I-1).** D54 keyed the default on the correction's type: `CORRECTION_BAND` for any `EquidistributionCorrection`, before `bind_correction` decided whether a layer moves. `setup` now takes it from the bound correction: `CORRECTION_BAND` (or the threshold if larger) when the closure's correction is a `LayerCorrection` with some nonzero gain, the threshold otherwise. An explicit `EquidistributionCorrection()` on a deck whose layers cannot vanish therefore deactivates as `NoCorrection` does, the relative-flux kernel installed with `c ≡ 0`, so C58's configuration cannot recur. The refusal of such a correction was not taken: it would break item 5(A)'s explicit-correction check on any class A deck with deactivation on. No table moves (no verification case passes an explicit correction; the ladder passes one on V-BO decks only, whose layers move).
- **D92. A merge's receiver is held to its own tolerances, by design (I-3).** The post-event test (`inadmissible`) holds the receiver's merged masses to the receiver's own `abstol`, which is what the domain test holds every later proposal to. Summing the merged cells' tolerances in the post-event test alone would pass an event whose next proposal the domain test must reject, ending in `DtLessThanMin` rather than the named `:Failure`; summing them in the integrator's tolerance vector would change every trajectory after an event. The docstrings of `inadmissible` and `solve` and the callback's comment say so; a test merges two near-depleted cells holding −0.6 and −0.3 times their gas tolerance with a receiver holding the same, the merged −1.8 inadmissible and −0.9 admissible, and pins `DomainCheck(cp).abstol === cp.abstol`.
- **D93. `vanishing` reads a self-yield by the rest of its products (C-1).** A product equal to the reactant is not counted among the reaction's products, and a reaction whose yield of its own reactant is one consumes nothing. The reversible chain and the reaction that can never run stay as they are, conservative and harmless. No deck has a self-yield, so no class moves.
- **D94. Every integration restarts the caloric warm start (X-3).** `integrate` resets `T_warm` to the set-up temperatures beside `reset_cells!`; the warm start inside a solve stays (C79). `setup` leaves `T_warm == T0` exactly in the inventory form, so every first integration is bit-identical. `residual!`'s docstring states the inventory form's dependence on the evaluation history at the inversion's round-off. The test (`test_policies.jl`) integrates one set-up problem twice around an evaluation at enthalpies 1 % above the initial ones, on a 30-cell ablator whose heat capacity rises with the temperature (with a constant heat capacity the inversion is linear and lands on the same root from any start, so the dependence does not show); on `8166e77` the second integration differs from the first, after the fix both equal a fresh setup's.
- **D95. The located event's run and the receiver's re-test, as the code does them (I-2, D-1).** `solve`'s docstring: at a located root the threshold and the band are widened by `reltol`, and the cells that go are the contiguous run from the exposed side that the widened tests take, which holds the crossing cell; a cell within `reltol` of its threshold inward of a cell above the band stays. The callback's comment: location takes a cell at its first downward crossing, so a cell that would have recovered by the step's end goes; only the end-of-step net misses a dip and recovery inside one step. `deactivate!`'s docstring: after a merge the receiver is tested in turn and, when it starts a run below the band with a cell below `ε_m`, merges on in the same invocation, against §3.4's one merge.
- **D96. Item 1 counts exactly one invocation per layer (S-1).** `item1_verdict` passes `invocations == layers` with the largest merged fraction at most 5 `ε_m`; the ladder's column passed `invocations <= layers`, so a run with no invocation read "pass". The column was recomputed from the csv (`burnout_ladder.jl --item1`, no solve): four rows go from pass to fail, V-BO-08 at `3e-2` with the correction (`:interior` at 67.04 s, C94's row) and V-BO-08's reference row without it (`:interior` at 68.86 s), in both forms. The second is not a matrix run of item 1; the gate says so beside it.
- **D97. One event-shift measure (S-3).** `test/newcore/gates/event_shift.jl` holds `invocation_times` (the new core's invocations at their first event's time, the old core's park times, none for a solution that records no events) and `event_shift` under D77's rule, included by `case_accuracy.jl` and `burnout_timing.jl`. On the new core the function is `case_accuracy.jl`'s, so item 7's numbers do not move; the timing table's shift column fills on its new-core rows. The first version dropped `case_accuracy.jl`'s guard for a solution without events, so the MMS cases' own solutions raised and their eight rows fell out of the table measured on `6bb9f18` (its other 92 rows equal to M6c's); `08de603` restores the guard, the eight rows equal M6c's again, and the table is measured on it.
- **D98. The stamps name the tree, and a table is measured on a signed commit (G-1).** `stamp.jl` prints `commit <hash> (tree <hash>)`; §9.3 gains the rule that a gate table is measured in a detached worktree of a signed commit, never of a commit still to be signed or rebased. Resolves C95.
- **D99. The sub-criteria no script measured (S-4).** Item 3: `energy_ledger.jl` gives V-BO-04's closure before its event a verdict per form against the old core's (the across-event table stays reported). Item 5(A): `burnout_identity.jl` records each moving deck's thinnest live `h/h₀` over both of its solves. Item 5(B): the largest activation and the most nodes active at one saved time, evaluated from the saved positions and the bound correction; V-BO-04 joins as a class B deck (C96). Item 6 is restated to D71's measure (§7.3). Item 8: `item8_verdict` reads every matched ratio as pass, owner or stop.
- **D100. Item 9 at reltol 1e-7 is a committed configuration (G-3).** C93's 1e-7 numbers came from a scratch run. `burnout_gradients.jl` runs its four configurations at the case's tolerance on both cores and again at reltol 1e-7 (abstol scaled with it) on the finite-volume core alone. Measured on `6bb9f18`: the rows at the case's tolerance are M6c's to every digit; at 1e-7 every configuration passes, the invocation time's spread 0.0039 (σ 2.8e-5 s) and 0.0079 (σ 2.2e-5 s) under the absorption coefficient at 60 and 120 cells, 0.00017 and 0.00041 under the flux and the pre-exponential factor, every other gated spread at most 0.034, one invocation and no fallback in every run (1,992 and 3,662 steps at ε = 0, against 1,127 and 2,118 at the case's tolerance).
- **D101. The installed correction under item 8's protocol, with its cost split at the first activation (X-1).** M6c timed the decks where the rule installs the correction (`ale` and `ale_darcy` at 25 and 100 cells with deactivation, V-CPL-02/02b) at equal tolerance, outside item 8's protocol. `burnout_timing.jl` now takes a deck descriptor, so those decks run the V-BO rows' protocol (each core's ladder against its own reference at 1e-9, the window before the first event of any solve, the whole run where none has one, the new core matched on the window RMS), beside the equal-tolerance ratio and the same deck with `NoCorrection()`. Per matched row whose correction activates, a read-only step recorder in a separate solve splits each core's time at `t_a`, the first saved time with a nonzero correction velocity: before it the correction is exactly zero, so the ratio there is the integrator's and the per-step cost's (R40), after it the gain `N²/τ` acts (R41), and the gain's share of the new core's time is `(T_new,after − r_before · T_old,after) / T_new`. One reported row runs `ale_darcy` 100 at `τ = 16 × 720` s, the per-node gain of `ale_darcy` 25 at the default. The scratch check at a load of 16 (V-BO-01 at 30 cells and `ale` 25) gave matched ratios 1.16 and 1.34, `t_a` 93.5 and 90.0 s, ratios 2.53 and 1.14 before and 0.99 and 1.39 after; the equal-gain row took 454 steps and two invocations. The table itself waits for a quiet machine (question 10 of the followup plan).

### 12.3 Conflicts found during implementation

- **C57. V-BO-04's exact law under the reactivity switch.** C46 says the law `L(t) = L₀ exp(−r t/ρ_s)` continues to hold through the event. On the new core the switch throttles every cell once it is below 1.5 `ε_m` (from 420.0 s), so the identical cells follow `dy/dt = −(r/ρ_s) s(y/(1.5 ε_m)) y` and reach `ε_m` at 465.5 s rather than 460.5 s, the thickness then lagging the plain law by several per cent. *Resolution taken:* on the new core the thickness QoI compares with the switched law, whose closed form is the primitive of `1/(x³(3 − 2x))`, up to the recorded event and with the survivor's own decay from the merged thickness after it (2.4e-7 and 1.2e-7 against 1e-4); the concentration QoI reads the cells with a finite temperature (the new core's outputs leave a dead cell's concentrations at zero, §6.1 item 14 being M3's); the live-cell QoI becomes one invocation, one survivor and 39 events; a new QoI bounds the event's delay behind the exact crossing at 1 % (the event follows the step until M5). The old core's QoIs are unchanged. *Owner:* confirm the re-pin, which question 8 approved for the plain law.
- **C58. The band without a correction changes an ablation case.** With `ε_band = 0.1` on every moving mesh, as §3.4 is written, V-CPL-02b (Landau ablation at half the activation energy, the wider reaction zone) deactivates 35 cells against 34, one invocation taking a near-threshold cell beneath the crossing one; its digest changes and its steady energy-balance QoI fails (2.35e-3 against 2e-3, 749 steps against 1,057). V-CPL-02 and V-BO-02 are unaffected. §3.4's two reasons for the band (the surface cell crossing first, C48, and the finite synchronization of an activated layer) both belong to the correction, and §7.3 item 5 requires V-CPL-02/02b bit-identical without one. *Resolution taken:* D54.
- **C59. The identity script misread the Douglas fir deck's reference.** The deck's name holds a comma and the reference reader split each line at every comma, so the deck read "no reference" although its digest equals the one recorded at M0 (`b2cab714cd3983a7`). The reader now splits at the last comma.
- **C60. The gas rows' absolute tolerance is the solid's.** `abstol_vector` (`src/States/tolerances.jl`) gives every mass row of a cell `reltol · ε_m · mˢᵢ(0)`, the scale of its condensed mass; on a gas component that is 2.6e-6 kg/m³ in Kaowool and 1.2e-5 in PMMA at reltol 1e-6, 10² to 10³ times the old core's 1e-8 kg/m³ (its scalar `abstol` on every row). A.9 loosened all mass rows together and so did not see it. Measured effects: (1) `KenCarp4`'s Newton on V-BO-02: in every one of its 1,599 divergences (θ > 2) a gas row held the largest scaled increment (median 918 times its weight, against 8e-7 for the energy rows and 3e-7 for the solid), in 903 of them the gas of the cell beneath the top live cell; with the gas rows scaled by the gas the cell's initial volume holds at its initial temperature and 1 atm (`ρ_g V₀`, a factor 1.5e-3 on PMMA and 7e-3 on Kaowool, about the old core's), `KenCarp4` with the smoothed estimate, the flag kept and the forced restart takes 1,494 steps and 61 Newton failures, the old core 1,572 and 56; (2) `Rodas5P`'s accuracy per tolerance on the audit decks: with the gas rows tightened a hundredfold it reaches the old default's error at reltol 1e-5 to 1e-6 instead of 1e-7, and every deck comes within 1.5× at matched accuracy (eulerian 100 1.48×, ale 25 1.13×, ale 100 1.38× against 1.90×, ale_darcy 25 0.99×, ale_darcy 100 1.28×), which is the cause of Phase 1's item 2 gap (C40); (3) on V-BO-02 the same tightening raises `Rodas5P`'s rejections at reltol 1e-6 from 953 to 1,533 and its matched time from 0.78 to 2.35× (`ρ_g V₀`: 2.05×). *Owner:* the scale (§12.4).
- **C61. The per-step derivative flag under `KenCarp4`.** With the flag raised (`:keep`, as the old core's step-update callback leaves it) the stage derivative at the start of each step is evaluated at the accepted state, where the stiff gas rows hold the Newton's leftover; with C60's weights that derivative is far off and the next Newton diverges (V-BO-02, smoothed estimate, forced restart: 1,036 failures and 6,467 steps; with the flag cleared 40 and 1,239). Clearing it costs `KenCarp4` 20 to 60× its accuracy on the finite slab (V-HC-03 at reltol 1e-8: 5.5e-3 K against the floor's 9.3e-5; V-HC-02c at 1e-6: 1.9e-2 against 5.9e-4), as Phase 0 found; with the gas rows tightened the flag can stay (V-BO-02: 59 failures, 1,436 steps).
- **C62. The solver-chosen restart under `KenCarp4`.** `kencarp4_policy` keeps the integrator's step through an event and the Newton then halves it about eleven times (651 of V-BO-02's 1,784 failures within 1e-4 s of an event; V-BO-01 with the correction: 36,009 failures, 226× the old core); the old core resets the step to `DT_AFTER_PARK` after every park. A Newton-based policy for deactivating solves takes the forced restart.
- **C63. V-BO-01's PMMA properties jump at 394 K.** The deck's fits are discontinuous there (c from 1,906 to 2,058 J/(kg·K), k from 0.160 to 0.176 W/(m·K)); every maximum of V-BO-01's window error lies on a PMMA cell between 396 and 405 K, and `KenCarp4` stays there at 0.18 to 0.30 K from reltol 3e-6 down on the new core and at 0.21 to 0.27 K from 1e-5 down on the old one, while `Rodas5P` converges (0.45 K at 1e-5 to 0.0096 K at 1e-7). The matched tolerance on V-BO-01 is set by stepping over the jump, `KenCarp4`'s at 3e-7 and `Rodas5P`'s at 1e-6.
- **C64. The correction's code is a cost, not a constant.** §5.7 says the prototype is enough for M2 because the lever is the integrator and the Jacobian, not the correction's code. As written, the prototype evaluated the node correction once per face in the relative-flux kernel and twice per node in the velocity rows, and reconstructed every face at `c = 0`: V-BO-01's residual was 59.9 µs at 300 s; without the correction it is 23 µs at 200 s, and the old core's right-hand side 18 µs there. Evaluated once per residual (D59) it is 31.8 µs, and V-BO-01 at reltol 1e-6 goes from 4.14 to 3.10× under `Rodas5P` and from 3.06 to 2.25× under `KenCarp4` (smoothed estimate, forced restart, flag cleared) between `burnout_timing.jl`'s two runs.
- **C65. The restart after an event under `Rodas5P` (question 7).** Measured on V-BO-02 at reltol 1e-6 in one process with the error before the first event unchanged (0.065 K, the restart cannot move it): the solver-chosen step 1.19 s, 1,706 steps, 953 rejections, ledger 1.5e-3; the forced 1e-6 s step 1.46 s, 2,790 steps, 286 rejections, ledger 7.6e-5 (A.6); the integrator's initial-step heuristic (`auto_dt_reset!`, two right-hand sides) 1.11 s, 2,014 steps, 351 rejections, ledger 4.6e-5, event shift 0.135 against 0.147. For `Rodas5P` the heuristic restart is faster than keeping the step and closes the ledger quantity C39 relaxed to 3e-3 within 1e-3. For `KenCarp4` (smoothed estimate, flag cleared) the forced restart stays best: 40 failures and 0.87 s against 899 and 1.28 s solver-chosen and 359 and 1.24 s with the heuristic. *Owner:* a third restart kind for `IntegratorPolicy` with question 7.
- **C66. The 30 colors are the reconstruction's, and one pass is not cheaper than two.** The color count is 30 whether the velocity rows reach the positions of nodes k−1..k+2 only, those and the masses of cells k−1..k+1, or those and the energies (4 and 60 cells; the positions-only pattern is contained at β = 0 and misses 40 entries at β = 1, so the mass columns are needed only with the monitor's mass term): the `RelativeFluxKernel`'s cell rows reaching i−2..i+2 set the count, as design §3.7's formula says, and C43's reach adds no color. A single dual pass of 30 partials costs 494 µs against 481 for two passes of 15 (`MAX_CHUNK`), the Jacobians equal: the cost is in the partials, so the chunk width is not a lever. Under the correction the colored Jacobian stays 1.9× the one without (481 against 257 µs at 60 cells); only fewer Jacobians or a narrower stencil (declined, R30) reduce it.
- **C67. Where `Rodas5P`'s rejections on V-BO-01 fall.** With the correction at reltol 1e-6: 245 of 1,335 proposals rejected by the error estimate and 52 by the domain test, every domain rejection coinciding with an error rejection, so C52's scale removes no step. Before 160 s (no event, the correction inactive) 152 rejections, 139 with the largest scaled error on the energy row of a PMMA cell crossing 394 K (C63): about 2.3 rejections per cell crossing, the same on the plain deck without the correction (144 of 477 proposals to 240 s, all energy rows) while the old core rejects 3 steps over the whole run; from 160 to 358 s 57 rejections, all on the top live cell's PMMA mass row (the burning surface); after the merge 36, on the top cell's gas and solid rows. A rejection costs a Jacobian and eight stages, about 0.8 ms, so the property jump costs about 7 % of the solve; the correction's own stiffness costs few rejections.
- **C68. The iteration matrix is factored on the Jacobian's template, explicit zeros included.** Timed where the integrator calls them over whole solves (every right-hand side, Jacobian, KLU analysis, refactorization, pivot-growth check, solve and `StructuredKLU` scatter; V-BO-01 at reltol 1e-6, quiet machine): a refactorization costs 157 to 163 µs on the new core against 36 on the old, a solve 20 against 9.8 and the pivot-growth check 34 against 11; no refactorization on either core fell back to fresh pivots. On V-BO-02, a fixed mesh without algebraic rows, the two cores' costs are equal (33 to 36 and 8.7 to 9.3 µs). The cause is the structure, not the pivots: `StructuredKLU` hands KLU the `jac_prototype`'s 27,904 stored entries, of which 4,200 to 6,700 are nonzero at a step, and KLU's factors hold 34,311 entries; a fresh analysis of the same structure gives the same fill and the same costs, one of a single `W`'s nonzeros 8,955. The union of the entries nonzero at any step of a solve is 8,200 (factors 11,950; refactorization 33 µs, solve 11.3 µs) and grows 76 to 172 times per solve, almost all after the tenth factorization. The old core's matrix carries the same kind of excess (8,887 stored against a union of 3,998), as does the new core's on V-BO-02 (8,704 against 3,200). A prototype that factors the union learned during the solve and analyses afresh whenever a new entry appears (scratch, `union_klu.jl`) takes 16 to 22 % off V-BO-01: `Rodas5P` 1.101 → 0.926 s at 1e-5 and 1.585 → 1.332 s at 1e-6, `KenCarp4` (smoothed estimate, forced restart, flag cleared) 1.199 → 0.930 s at 1e-6 and 1.547 → 1.264 s at 3e-7, with 76 to 159 analyses per solve; the window measures of D58 and C70 are equal to three digits, and the two paths' temperatures agree within 6e-4 K (`KenCarp4`) and 5e-2 K (`Rodas5P`) before the first event and differ after it by an event-time shift (D58). The gate table's unit costs factor a freshly analysed `W` at 200 s (`_klu_costs`), so they missed this, and with them the projection of the remaining levers to 1.8 to 2.3×. *Owner:* adopted as the package's sparse factorization (§12.4).
- **C69. The gas rows' tolerance is a dial, and no setting helps everywhere.** C60's `ρ_g V₀` is `reltol · ε_m · ρ_g(T₀, 1 atm) V₀`. Written `reltol · s · ρ_g V₀`, C60's scale is `s = ε_m = 1e-2`, and `s = 1` resolves the gas of a fully porous cell at ambient to reltol, 6.7× (PMMA) and 1.4× (Kaowool) tighter than the current scale. Matched by D58's window maximum (`burnout_timing.jl`'s measures, scratch `matrix.jl`, `vbo_gas.jl`, `audit_gas.jl`), at the current scale and `s` of 1, 0.1 and 1e-2: on V-BO-02, `Rodas5P` 0.80, 0.59, 2.10 and 1.88× (with the heuristic restart 0.84, 0.85, 1.69 and 1.57×), `KenCarp4` with the flag cleared 0.84, 0.91, 1.03 and 1.13×, with it kept 1.62, 1.28 and 1.38× at the three `s`. At `s = 1e-2` and reltol 1e-6, 988 of `Rodas5P`'s 1,230 error rejections fall on gas rows, 742 of them in the top live cell and the one beneath it, and 989 of all 1,230 fall within twenty proposals of an event: the tolerance makes the integrator resolve the venting of the newly exposed cell after each of the sixty events, which no measured quantity needs (its window error is 0.19 K at 1e-5 against the target's 0.375). On V-BO-01, tightening lets `Rodas5P` match at a looser tolerance (1.64 s at the current scale and 1e-6, 1.35 s at `s = 0.1` and 3e-6, 1.31 s at `s = 1e-2` and 1e-5) and does not help `KenCarp4` (1.55 to 1.80 s at 3e-7), which with the flag kept matches nowhere on the ladder at `s = 1` and at 3.2× at `s = 1e-2`. On the audit decks (`Rodas5P`, temperature form, matched, the four settings in order): eulerian 100 1.40, 1.61, 1.75, 1.69×; ale 25 1.44, 1.06, 1.21, 1.24×; ale 100 1.86, 1.70, 1.51, 1.46×; ale_darcy 25 1.05, 1.15, 1.19, 1.42×; ale_darcy 100 1.38, 1.57, 1.19, 1.95×. No setting brings every deck within 1.5×, so C60's effect (2) is not reproduced: the tighter rows buy accuracy per tolerance and pay for it in steps. *Owner:* the scale is kept (§12.4).
- **C70. Matched accuracy on V-BO-01 depends on the measure and on the old core's time.** The old core's own window error does not converge (0.247, 0.271, 0.263, 0.206 and 0.247 K at reltol 1e-5 to 1e-7), so the target is the old core's floor; `KenCarp4` on the new core floors at 0.18 to 0.30 K, and neither falls with the tolerance when every sample within 10 K of the 394 K jump is left out (the old core 0.14 to 0.26 K, `KenCarp4` 0.18 to 0.42 K). Matched in one run (old default 0.655 s) by the window maximum, that maximum without the jump's samples, the RMS over the window's cells and saved times, and the window's mass-loss-rate L1 error: `Rodas5P` 2.50× (1e-6), 1.76× (1e-5), 1.76× (1e-5) and 3.74× (3e-7); `KenCarp4` 2.49× (3e-7) on the first three and 3.31× (1e-7) on the last. The old default's warm time was 0.564 to 0.655 s over four runs on a quiet machine and 0.561 s in the gate table at a load of 10: a spread of ±8 % in the denominator of every ratio. *Owner:* item 8 matches on the RMS, over the median of more old-core solves (§12.4).
- **C71. Before the first event the correction's machinery doubles the cost.** V-BO-01 to 160 s at reltol 3e-7 in one process (the same run's 1e-6 block is discarded: its old-core right-hand side timed 27.7 µs against 15 to 16 µs in every other run): the old core 0.117 s (110 steps); without the correction (`c = 0`, 18 colors) `KenCarp4` 0.186 s (145 steps) and `Rodas5P` 0.747 s (601 steps, 483 rejections, the 394 K jump of C67); with it (30 colors) `KenCarp4` 0.365 s (201 steps; a Jacobian 483 against 294 µs, a refactorization 156 against 78 µs) and `Rodas5P` 1.227 s (725 steps). The correction is not inert before its first activation (39 % more steps under `KenCarp4`); why is open. *Resolved at M3:* the activation turns on long before the first event. V-BO-01 to 160 s at reltol 3e-7 on the package's correction (scratch `c71.jl`): with an activation that cannot be reached (`activation = (1e-6, 1e-7)`: the kernel installed and 28 colors, `c ≡ 0`), `Rodas5P` takes 613 steps, 482 rejections, 613 Jacobians and 9,373 right-hand sides and `KenCarp4` (smoothed estimate, forced restart, flag cleared) 145 steps, 5 rejections, 26 Jacobians and 3,687 right-hand sides, exactly the counts without a correction, at 0.667 against 0.595 s and 0.146 against 0.139 s (the wider Jacobian). The package's correction activates at 92.5 s (cell 180, the absorption peak, at 0.299 of its thickness; 9 active nodes at 120 s, 17 at 160 s), 75 s before the old core's first park at 167.8 s, and from then the steps rise: `Rodas5P` 728 (+19 %), `KenCarp4` 200 (+38 %). After 160 s the whole solves of `matrix.jl` at 3e-7 spend 0.64 s (old core, its parks), 1.26 s (`KenCarp4`) and 1.22 s (`Rodas5P`). The fallback's `c = 0` halves the cost before the first event, but its burnout phase needs the interior merge (C54), which is unbuilt; V-BO-02, a fixed mesh at `c = 0` with sixty events, runs at 0.8× the old core.
- **C72. A monitor mean over the current state makes the rows dense.** §3.2 normalizes the monitor by its mean over the layer. Taken at the current state, that mean reads every cell's masses in the layer, so every node correction, and through them every balance row and every cell row of the layer, would reach every cell of it, against §3.6's banded reach (the coloring would then need a color per column of the layer). *Resolution taken:* the mean of the initial state (`LayerCorrection.monitor_scale`), `M̃ = M/⟨M(0)⟩`. On V-BO-01's layer, of uniform density, it is one at every time; on a charring layer it lets the monitor's overall scale drift with the density, which the default `β = 0` (`M̃ = 1`) does not see and V-BO-07 (M6) measures.
- **C73. The relative fluxes beside a boundary cell are not positivity-preserving.** Where no cell lies beyond the upwind cell (the cell at the pinned end, or the top cell when the flow leaves it inward), the face state is the linear interpolation toward the downwind cell without a limiter (Phase 1's `_face_value`). A cell holding almost none of a component beside a steep gradient of it is then drained below zero. On an inert two-solid deck (20 cells on a stretched mesh relaxing under the correction, a tanh front leaving 0.13 kg/m³ of the second solid in the back cell), that mass crossed −abstol at 2.2 s and the domain test rejected every proposal after it (`DtLessThanMin`). Phase 1's prescribed path does the same: the slide of `test_relative_flux.jl` on its two-solid deck at 8 cells instead of 20 drains the top cell's 0.158 kg/m³ of the first solid and stops at 2.59 s, while at 20 cells the smallest mass stays above 1,900 times its tolerance. V-BO-01 does not meet it: its Lagrangian interface holds still and its layer's composition is uniform. *Resolution taken:* none in M3, since §3.3 keeps the kernel and a limited slope there would change the prescribed decks' digests; the advection test keeps both solids above a quarter of the mass. *Owner:* whether to limit that slope (the van Albada slope against a zero gradient, or first order beside the boundary).
- **C74. At an active node with `c = 0` the upwind side follows the dual seeds.** The relative fluxes change their upwind side at `u = 0`, a kink (§3.3). ForwardDiff 1.4.6 orders two dual numbers of equal value by their partials, so the test `u ≥ 0` at a `u` that is exactly zero with nonzero partials (an active node with equal `M̃h` on both sides, as between uniform cells) takes the side of the first nonzero partial, which differs between the colored passes and the dense one. On the two-layer containment deck at a heated state with a uniform substrate, the energy rows of the two substrate cells beside such a node read 766 and 2,190 colored against 703 and 2,254 dense, each a one-sided derivative along its seeds; at the same state with the nodes moved by up to 5 % the two Jacobians agree to the last bit. A solve meets the tie only on a uniform activated region, where the flux of a uniform composition does not see the kink. *Resolution taken:* the containment states move every interior node by up to 5 %. *Owner:* whether the kernel should take the side from the value alone, so that both passes take the same one-sided derivative (unmeasured on the identity decks).
- **C75. V-BO-03's layer is still live at its end on the new core.** With the substrate transparent, the flux the thinning film transmits leaves through the back face, and the correction keeps the film's cells together: at 500 s they hold about 1.5 % of their mass (mass loss 68.88 % at 60 cells against the layer's 69.91 %), above `ε_m`, so no cell has deactivated, where the old core, parking one cell at a time, has parked 24, 48 and 96 of 30, 60 and 120. The case's four QoIs pass as pinned (its mass loss is informational, at 10 points), the peak and its time equal the old core's at each resolution (30.394 against 30.4 at 316.5 s with 30 cells), and the mass loss rates differ by at most 0.12 % of the peak over the whole run; item 1 reads zero invocations. *Resolution taken:* none; the case asserts what it pinned. *Owner:* whether M6 extends V-BO-03's end so that item 1 sees its layer's invocation. *Measured at M6b:* the layer deactivates in one invocation of all its cells at 3,022.46, 3,022.87 and 3,022.97 s at 30, 60 and 120 cells, both forms within 3 ms, every cell at the threshold together (the largest merged fraction 1.0002 %, the located root's). The film holds 1.48 % of its mass at 500 s, 1.17 % at 1,000 s and 1.04 % at 2,000 s, its decay slowing as the thinning film absorbs less. The gate rows run to 3,100 s (D88).
- **C76. R25 measured: the end-of-layer burst is the merged temperature, not the switch.** At `ε_m = 1e-2` (temperature form; the enthalpy form within 1 %), with `R0 = Σ mᵢ k(Tᵢ) sᵢ` before the merge, `Ra = m_res k(T̄) s̄`, `Rb = m_res k(T̄)` and `Rc = m_res k(T_m) s_m` after it (`burnout_ladder.md`): the switch's release `Rb/Ra` is 1.04, 1.03 and 1.04 at 30, 60 and 120 cells (`s̄` 0.96 to 0.97; the largest merged fraction 3.1 to 3.4 %), and the merged temperature's `Rc/Rb` is 1.31, 1.09 and 1.03. The receiver is the top Kaowool cell, which absorbs the flux the thin film transmits and stands 25 to 60 K above the film's mass-weighted temperature (782, 758 and 747 K against 722 K); its share of the merged heat capacity, `(T_m − T̄)/(T_receiver − T̄)`, is 0.121, 0.061 and 0.032, halving with the cell, and lifts `T_m` 7.2, 2.2 and 0.8 K above `T̄`. The merged rate is 34.4, 29.6 and 28.4 g m⁻² s⁻¹ against 26.2, 27.3 and 27.7 just before the merge, and the mass gasified from the event on equals the residue (140 against 140 g/m² at 30 cells). Without the switch the burst stays (`Rc/Rb` 1.28 at 30 cells; peak 35.11 at 359.0 s against 35.42 at 359.5 s with it), and at 60 and 120 cells the runs without it pass every QoI with the same steps (1,126 at 60 cells). At `ε_m = 3e-3` the residue is a fifth as large (29 to 33 g/m²) and the receiver's share larger (0.39, 0.23, 0.13), so the jump is larger (`Rc/Rb` 2.30, 1.31, 1.09; 47.6 g m⁻² s⁻¹ at 30 cells), but the burst lasts under a second and falls between the 0.5 s saved times (the largest saved rate after it 29.9 at 364.0 s), so the point-sampled peak passes. At `τ = 360` s the cells reach the threshold deeper in the switch (`s̄` 0.82): the release is then 1.21 and the temperature part 1.15, and the burst (33.0 at 361.5 s) exceeds the main peak (32.39 at 335.0 s) and moves `t_peak` in the temperature form (30.43 at 362.0 s in the enthalpy form, which passes). R25's last sentence has it backwards: merging the layer's last invocation into its innermost cell rather than the substrate cell would remove the temperature part (that cell is at the film's temperature), not the switch part, and would leave the merged cell, holding the residue, to deactivate in a second invocation. *Owner:* the switch (question 12) and the 30-cell row (question 8), §12.4.
- **C77. Item 2's curve criterion measures the burnout instant, which neither core resolves to 3 %.** Over the saved times from the old core's peak on, every V-BO-01 run differs from the old core at its resolution by 7.2 to 39.5 % of the peak, always between 359.0 and 365.0 s, and the old core differs from itself at twice the cells by 18.4 % (30 against 60) and 6.8 % (60 against 120), at 365.0 s; V-BO-03, which does not burn out by its end, meets it (0.025 to 0.12 %). Ending the window earlier (scratch `curve_window.jl`, temperature form, `ε_m = 1e-2`): up to the old core's burnout time the new core differs by 19.9, 13.2 and 8.1 % at 30, 60 and 120 cells, at 359.0 to 360.0 s, the layer's invocation (the old core against itself 8.5, 1.8 and 1.8 %); up to 5 s before it, by 0.56, 0.56 and 1.20 % (the old core against itself 2.4, 1.6 and 0.7 %). Before the merge the two cores' rates agree as closely as the old core agrees with its own refinement; the difference is the last 5 s, where the new core gasifies the layer's last 2 % in one burst after its invocation and the old core in sixty parks. *Proposed:* restate the criterion over the saved times from the old core's peak to 5 s before its burnout time (pass at 3 %), and judge the burst by item 2's peak and its time, which see it whenever it exceeds the main peak (C76). *Owner.*
- **C78. At `ε_m = 3e-2` the layer deactivates in two invocations.** With the band at 0.1 (3.3 `ε_m`) the first cell crosses the threshold at 309 to 312 s, before the main peak, while most of the layer is still above 10 % of its mass: the first invocation takes 17, 35 and 69 of 30, 60 and 120 cells (the largest merged fraction 9.0 to 9.95 %, the band's limit, within 5 `ε_m`), into a PMMA receiver at the film's temperature (no temperature part, `Rc/R0` 1.04), and the second takes the rest at about 359 s. Every other QoI passes at 60 and 120 cells; at 30 cells the second invocation's burst fails the peak and `t_peak` (38.66 at 360.5 s). The prototype's two invocations at 3e-2 (A.4) were the same row with a band of 10 `ε_m`. `τ = 3600` s also ends in two (34 cells at 302 s, 26 at the end), the layer under-relaxed as in A.10. Item 1 forbids widening the band on the ladder, and a shorter `τ` synchronizes harder at the cost of `C76`'s switch release. *Owner:* §7.4, §12.4.
- **C79. In the enthalpy form a solve depends on evaluations outside the integrator.** `derive!` starts each cell's caloric inversion from `T_warm`, the temperatures of the latest plain evaluation, which the workspace shares (Phase 1's warm start), and the inversion stops within its round-off of the root, so an evaluation the integrator did not make, a step recorder's, moves the later inversions and the trajectory at round-off. V-BO-01, 02 and 03 at 60 cells through the case's solve, whose recorder evaluates every accepted state, against the same solves without it: 1,571, 2,794 and 1,576 steps against 1,589, 2,822 and 1,544, every QoI passing either way at the same values to their printed digits (V-BO-01's peak 32.391, ledger 2.2e-5); the temperature form, whose temperature is a state, gives equal digests. M3's enthalpy counts came through a recorder as well. Consequences: a bit-identity check in the enthalpy form compares solves with the same evaluations, and the ladder's enthalpy wall times are of plain solves that part from the instrumented ones at round-off. *Resolution taken:* none; the inversion's result is its root to round-off. *Owner:* whether the inversion should start from a guess the state alone determines (the bracket's linear estimate), at the cost of its iterations, so that the enthalpy form, too, is a function of the state.
- **C80. The correction is active on decks items 5 and 8 take to leave it inactive.** Installed at its defaults (`burnout_timing.md`, reltol 1e-6 on the audit decks, the case's on V-CPL-02/03), it stays at exactly zero on `ale` at 25 cells and on V-CPL-03, whose solves are then identical to those without it at 1.21 and 1.20× the time (the wider pattern), but activates on `ale` at 100 cells (largest correction velocity 1.9e-6 m/s, the temperatures moved by up to 0.48 K, 71 steps against 54), on `ale_darcy` at 25 and 100 cells (1.4e-5 and 1.6e-5 m/s, 5.2 and 4.6 K, 156 steps against 100 and 430 against 102, the last 5.2× the time) and on V-CPL-02 (5.7e-4 m/s, 15.8 K, 1,122 steps against 2,049, 1.14× the time). Each of these decks gasifies its surface cells entirely (the PMMA and the audit wood leave no residue; Landau's ablation removes the cell), so its surface cells thin below `r_hi = 0.3` of their initial thickness and the correction, one layer over the whole slab, equidistributes them as designed. Item 8's "present but inactive" cost is therefore measured where it is inactive, and item 5's expectation that the largest activation stays exactly zero holds for V-CPL-03 and not for V-CPL-02 (V-CPL-02b, V-CPL-06's column, V-RX-02d, V-HC-05, V-RAD-01 and the Douglas fir deck are M6's). The default correction of `setup` is `NoCorrection` (§10), so no case changes. *Owner:* whether item 5 runs the vanishing decks with the correction and expects its activation, and the thresholds from a material's residual fraction (D69), which would keep the correction off a char former's cells. *Owner (§14):* the temperature differences above compare cells by index after the mesh has moved and are not errors; against references at four times the cells the correction is neutral or better (Appendix C.3). Item 5 splits by installation (§7.3): the rule installs the correction on the vanishing decks with deactivation, where its activation is recorded, and D69 is dropped.
- **C81. Passing only the located cell ends V-BO-04 `:interior`.** V-BO-04 runs without a correction, so its band is `ε_m`. Its forty identical cells cross within round-off of one another; the margin's root falls on whichever crosses first, an interior cell, while the cells outward of it sit about 1e-15 above their thresholds, so the run from the exposed side is empty and the crossing cell lies inward of live cells: the solve ended `:interior` at 465.49 s with no event. The end-of-step event never meets this, since by a step's end every cell is below. *Resolution taken:* D76.
- **C82. Location cannot see a margin that is already zero.** DiffEqBase fires `affect_neg!` only when the condition's previous sign is positive, so a cell whose margin is exactly zero at the start of a step, as a root non-strictly at the threshold would leave it, is never located (the prototype lost 21 of 60 events that way without its safety net, A.5). D76's slack takes the crossing cell at every root, and the end-of-step event takes a cell that starts a step at its threshold; `test_deactivation.jl` starts a solve so.
- **C83. After a located event the step's interpolant is gone.** `change_t_via_interpolation!` moves the step's end to the root and its `dt` with it before the event modifies the state, so neither the post-event state nor the interpolant gives the integrand just before the event. A ledger that integrates over accepted steps takes that node itself (D79).
- **C84. Two of item 5's identity decks deactivate.** V-CPL-02 and 02b move with location on by default (D78); with `located_events = false` they reproduce the reference digests (`5e0df61c6838c7e5`, `79a0ab788c4427ee`), and the other six decks build no event callback. *Resolution taken:* none; `burnout_identity.jl` keeps its reference. *Owner:* whether item 5 re-records those two digests at the package defaults (D62's precedent, the end-of-step digests kept here as the link to Phase 1), or the identity script solves them with location off. *Owner:* re-recorded at the package defaults when W1 deletes the switch (§14.8, question 21).
- **C90. A test deck's threshold thickness equals the default `min_thickness`.** `test_deactivation.jl`'s located-event test solves a two-cell, 0.2 mm ablator: the cells' threshold thickness `ε_m h₀` is 1 µm, the default `min_thickness`, so the end-of-step solve ended at that callback's root with the last cell at its threshold to round-off, and whether a last event fired was the round-off's. The switch deletion changed that round-off, and the assertion that every end-of-step event leaves its cell below the threshold failed on the last cell (0.009999999999999768). *Resolution taken:* the test's solves pass `min_thickness = 1e-8`, so they end by deactivation.
- **C91. V-BO-07 fails item 1's merged-fraction clause.** At its CI resolution the mixed layer deactivates in one invocation of its 60 cells, at 314.09 s with `β = 0` (the largest merged fraction 5.82 %) and at 312.38 s with `β = 1` (6.07 %), the same in both forms, against 5 `ε_m` = 5 % (R46). The burn runs down from the surface: by 230 s the upper cells have lost their PMMA and hold only the slow solid at 9 to 14 % of their mass, while the interface cell, nearly pure PMMA, keeps 76 % at 280 s and falls to 1 % by 314 s, faster than any relaxation. At the invocation the fraction rises monotonically from 1.2 % at the interface to 5.8 % at the surface, every cell holding only the slow solid, and the thickness is far from equidistributed (`h/h₀` 0.020 to 0.078). The correction relaxes thickness (`β = 0`) or thickness and density (`β = 1`), while the threshold reads each cell's mass against its own initial mass, and here two kinetics are graded in space. Neither `τ` (360, 180 and 90 s: 6.32, 6.15 and 4.67 % with `β = 0`; 7.06, 7.91 and 5.84 % with `β = 1`) nor the mass term brings it within the clause at the defaults. With the second solid at PMMA's kinetics, so that only the density varies, the layer goes in one invocation at 2.92 % (`β = 0`) and 2.37 % (`β = 1`): the deck as §6.2 specifies it fails on its graded kinetics, not on its density. The same residue shows in item 2's reported burst measures (`burnout_burst.md`): V-BO-07's per-step excess of N over 2N cells is 1.61, 0.80 and 1.99 g m⁻² s⁻¹ (stopping to fall at 120 → 240) against the old core's 12.0, 3.67 and 0.90, while its successive L1 falls 1.99 and 1.93× per doubling. *Resolution taken:* the case asserts the one invocation, which is §6.2's acceptance for V-BO-07, and reports the largest merged fraction (`_vbo_invocation_qoi`'s `merged_limit = nothing`); item 1 reads the clause in the ladder, where both V-BO-07 rows fail it, and the band is not widened. *Owner:* (a) an owner exception of item 1 on V-BO-07, the mechanism named, as the 3e-2 row's (recommended: the clause bounds the lump a merge makes, and here the lump is the slow solid's residue, which the deck builds in); (b) V-BO-07's second solid at PMMA's kinetics, which passes and tests what §6.2 names, the density inside one activated region; or (c) a threshold or monitor that reads each cell's remaining reactivity, design work beyond Phase 2.
- **C92. The old core's ledger on V-BO-06.** On the graded mesh the old core's mass ledger reads 3.8e-3, 4.1e-3 and 4.0e-3 at 0.5, 0.1 and 0.02 s saves, against the case's 1e-3, so it is not the trapezoid's error. The gap builds during the first parks, of the thin surface cells: by 200 s, after 11 parks, the outflow exceeds the mass lost by 5.2e-3 of the total drop, and the gap stays there to the end (on V-BO-01's uniform mesh 8.6e-4 by 200 s and 7.5e-4 at the end, at 0.1 s saves). The finite-volume core closes the same deck to 1.0e-4. *Resolution taken:* V-BO-06's ledger tolerance is 5e-3 on the old core and 1e-3 on the finite-volume core, as V-BO-02's is core-dependent; the old core is not changed. *Owner:* confirm.
- **C93. Item 9's invocation time is noise-limited at the case's tolerance.** On the final defaults the flux and the pre-exponential factor pass; under the PMMA absorption coefficient the invocation time's spread is 0.143 at 60 cells and 0.128 at 120 (its differences 2.98, 2.57, 2.97 and 2.96 s, and 3.31, 3.08, 2.92 and 2.96 s, per unit `ln α`), against 0.1, while the invocation count is one in every run, no run falls back, and the peak, its time and the burnout time are smooth (0.0006 to 0.041). The review's 0.086 at 60 cells was measured with the switch (`768944e`). The invocation time is the least sensitive quantity, about 3 s per unit `ln α` against 19 to 34 s for the timed ones, so the step noise (σ = 5.5e-4 and 3.7e-4 s; h10 1.9e-3 and 1.3e-3) is 10 to 20 % of a central difference at h = 1e-3 and 2e-3. At reltol 1e-7 (the same script, scratch) σ falls to 2.8e-5 and 2.2e-5 s and the spreads to 0.0039 and 0.0079, and both configurations pass. The old core's first park is erratic on every configuration (spreads 1.4 to 109). *Resolution taken:* none; item 9 reads as failing on two of its four configurations at the case's tolerance. *Owner:* (a) item 9 passes with the noise named: the count never changes, nothing falls back, and the spread falls with the tolerance, so a user who differences the invocation time at a relative step below 2e-3 sets reltol 1e-7 (recommended); (b) restate the criterion on h10, the relative step at which the noise makes a central difference 10 % wrong (1.3e-3 to 1.9e-3 here, every other gated quantity at most 5.3e-4); or (c) read item 9 at reltol 1e-7, a change of Phase 0's protocol.
- **C94. V-BO-08 at `ε_m = 3e-2` ends `:interior`.** The ladder's run of the thin layer at the largest threshold stops at 67.04 s with no invocation, in both forms: a cell beneath the surface reaches 3 % of its mass while a cell above it holds more than the band. The layer's activated life is short: its first cell crosses `r_hi = 0.3` of its thickness at about 55 s (the first active step at 56.05 s) and the layer burns out at 69 s, against the correction's synchronization time `τ/π²` = 73 s at the default `τ = 720` s; §13 question 3's heuristic asks for at most a tenth of the layer's life after activation (R27). At 3e-3 and 1e-2 the layer goes in one invocation, at 1.48 and 4.53 % against 1.5 and 5 % (R46). The ladder's item 1 column counts at most one invocation per layer, so it reads this row as passing; the row fails item 1 and its case's QoIs. *Resolution taken:* none. *Owner:* (a) an owner exception of item 1 on V-BO-08 at 3e-2, as V-BO-01's 3e-2 row, the mechanism named (recommended for Phase 2: the default threshold passes); (b) a layer time that scales with the layer (`τ` from its thickness and regression rate, R27's lever), design work for the cutover, which would also tighten V-BO-08's margins at the default.
- **C95. Seven tables, the gate and plan §12.1 stamped with a commit that does not exist.** M6c's tables (`burnout_ladder`, `burnout_burst`, `burnout_identity`, `case_accuracy`, `energy_ledger`, `burnout_gradients`, `burnout_timing`), the gate's scope and §12.1's M6c and M7 rows name `5f08134`, which is not an object of the repository: `stamp.jl` read HEAD before the commit queue was replayed with `git am -S`, which changed every hash. It is the pre-signing hash of `78ce699` ("Time the correction where the installation rule installs it"), whose tree `543bbb8` the tables measured; `git diff 78ce699..8166e77` touches documents and tables only, so every table describes `8166e77`'s code. The mapping lived only in the gitignored `docs/refs/phase2_commit_queue/TREES.txt`. *Resolution taken:* D98; the mapping is written into the gate's scope and §12.1, and the tables the followup did not re-measure (`burnout_burst`) keep their stamp, read through it.
- **C96. V-BO-04's correction is round-off, not exactly zero.** §7.3 item 5(B) and §2.5 of the followup plan expect V-BO-04's correction to be exactly zero, its column being uniform; M4 and M6a read it so from equal step counts with and without it. Measured directly (`burnout_identity.jl`, its CI resolution, temperature form): the largest correction velocity is 6.75e-18 m/s, nonzero at 5,686 of 9,491 node and saved-time entries from 122.0 s on, 6.8e-14 of the surface's regression speed; the solve takes the same 322 accepted steps and 0 rejected as with `NoCorrection()`, its temperatures within 1.8e-12 K and its masses within 4.2e-14 relative, but its digest differs (`4e3cf8a83e9e9078` against `2156761ec2e00005`). The uniform column thins every cell alike, so `h_out − h_in` is the round-off of the node positions, and every interior node activates (39 at once). *Resolution taken:* none; the table reports the measured value. *Owner:* (a) restate item 5(B)'s clause as "zero to round-off: at most 1e-12 of the surface speed, on the same accepted steps as without the correction" (recommended: the column is uniform only to round-off, and nothing downstream reads the difference); (b) keep "exactly zero" and make the activation or the correction exactly zero on equal thicknesses, a code change to the correction for a property of one test deck.
- **C97. K-1 does not reach V-CPL-02 or V-CPL-02b.** The review found the K-1 path reachable on every class B deck after its first event, and the followup plan expected V-CPL-02 and V-CPL-02b's identity digests to change with D90, their reference re-recorded (§14.6 row 6) and their `case_accuracy.jl` rows new. Measured on the parent and the fix (both forms): both decks are bit-identical, with the same QoI values (D90). At no saved state after a first event is the receiver's inner node's correction nonzero: the receiver takes the dead cells' volume and stays above the activation bound `r_hi`, so its inner node has `c = 0` exactly and the kernel skips the face. The path is reached where a receiver ends below `r_hi` and thicker than its inner neighbour: `ale_darcy` at 25 and 100 cells with deactivation (85 and 78 saved states with a positive correction there), whose trajectories change (585 to 583 and 1,620 to 1,623 steps). *Resolution taken:* the class B reference is not re-recorded for D90 (only V-BO-04 joins it, D99), `case_accuracy.jl` is re-run as a check of S-3 and of the followup's code (its V-CPL-02/02b rows reproduce), and the `ale_darcy` rows of item 8 carry the change. *Owner:* none needed; recorded because the plan's expectation and the review's reach claim were wrong.

### 12.4 Next

M2 is closed: with the owner's decisions below in place (D61 to D64), V-BO-01 runs at 1.47 to 1.64× the old core over four runs (matched on the window RMS) and V-BO-02 at 0.60×, and the owner accepted the burnout path at about 1.5×. Where V-BO-01's matched solve goes (`burnout_timing.md`): the colored Jacobian 38 to 39 % (767 evaluations at 443 to 480 µs; of one evaluation, the dual residual's `derive!` 31 %, its kernels 28 %, its closure rows 9 %, the partials' extraction 16 %), the right-hand side 22 to 24 % (22.4 against the old core's 15.5 µs: `derive!` 8.7, the relative-flux kernel 4.3), the formation of the iteration matrix 5 to 14 %, the factorization and the solves 15 to 17 %, the domain test 2 to 10 %. On both burnout decks and on the Douglas fir deck (A.8) the gap is one cost: `Rodas5P` builds an exact Jacobian every step, where the old core builds an approximate one every five or so; the new core's steps, right-hand sides and linear algebra are at or below the old core's.

**Carried to the phase gate** (§7.3 item 8 is re-measured there on the package's correction, M4 and M6), in the order to try them: (i) a Jacobian-reusing integrator at matched accuracy, `FBDF` first (`fbdf_policy` exists and was never measured on V-BO-01 or the Douglas fir deck; `KenCarp4` failed for C60 and C61, the Rosenbrock-W methods for A.7); (ii) forming `W` in the factorization's union directly (a `Wfact` of the package's) instead of OrdinaryDiffEq's sparse broadcast over the 27,904-entry template, about 8 to 9 % of V-BO-01; (iii) the remaining absent-work skips, first the emissivity and the absorptivity of every cell, which only the exposed cells read (about 6 % of a Jacobian); (iv) element-local Jacobians (each kernel's rows differentiated on its own stencil and assembled) in place of the colored dual passes, the structural lever and a project of weeks, which the multi-D extension needs too; (v) the compact relative-flux stencil, declined (R30), about 15 % of V-BO-01 if reopened. R40 holds the risk.

**M3 is done** (§12.1): the package's `Correction` types, `correction!` and `ds.c` replace the prototype's, the decks without a correction are bit-identical, and V-BO-01 ends in one invocation at 30, 60 and 120 PMMA cells, with every QoI at 60 and 120 cells in both forms. C71 is resolved: the activation turns on at 92.5 s, 75 s before the old core's first park, and the machinery alone (the kernel and the 28 colors) changes no step.

**M4's measurements are taken** (§12.1). V-BO-01 ends in one invocation at `ε_m` of 3e-3 and 1e-2 at 60 and 120 cells with every QoI of item 2 in both forms, and in two at 3e-2 (C78), so §7.4's condition fails on the 3e-2 row alone, and the phase waits for the owner's decision there. The decisions, with recommendations:

1. **§7.4 and the 3e-2 row (C78).** *Recommendation:* M5 to M7 proceed, the 3e-2 row recorded as an owner exception of item 1 (two invocations; every other QoI passes at 60 and 120 cells). The default threshold and 3e-3 pass, and tuning within M4's allowance has no candidate: item 1 forbids widening the band on the ladder, a shorter `τ` deepens the switch's release (C76: 360 s fails `t_peak`), and moving the activation acts on every row. The fallback (item 11) would replace a path that passes at the default with one built on the unverified merge of C54. *Changes:* with "the fallback", C54's merge (about one and a half weeks) before M5. *Owner:* as recommended; the 3e-2 row is an owner exception of item 1.
2. **The reactivity switch (question 12, C76).** *Recommendation:* keep it. The burst is the merged temperature, not the switch (its release ×1.03 to 1.07 at the default `τ`); without it no QoI of the ladder changes its verdict and V-BO-01 at 60 cells takes the same 1,126 steps; and V-CPL-02/02b are pinned with it (C27). *Changes:* with "drop it", `switch_width = 0` under an equidistribution correction and C27's V-CPL-02 numbers re-run. *Owner:* as recommended; the switch stays. *Superseded by the decision review (§14):* the switch is deleted (W1).
3. **The 30-cell row (question 8, C76).** *Recommendation:* an owner exception on the peak and its time at 30 cells for `ε_m` of 1e-2 and 3e-2, the mechanism named: the merged residue takes the substrate cell's temperature in proportion to that cell's share of the merged heat capacity, which halves with the cell. The main peak itself is right at 30 cells (32.248 at 333.0 s at 3e-3, the old core's 32.254 at 333.0 s). *Changes:* with "merge into the layer", a layer's last invocation merges into its innermost cell, which removes the burst and adds a second invocation per layer to item 1. *Owner:* as recommended; the peak and its time at 30 cells for `ε_m` of 1e-2 and 3e-2 are an owner exception. *Superseded by the decision review (§14):* the exception is withdrawn; the peak is read on the main peak (C86) and the burst is judged by item 2's refinement criteria (W4).
4. **The curve criterion (C77).** *Recommendation:* restate it over the saved times from the old core's peak to 5 s before its burnout time, at 3 % (0.56 to 1.20 % measured at `ε_m = 1e-2`), the burst judged by item 2's peak and its time. *Changes:* §7.3 item 2 and `burnout_ladder.jl`'s curve measure. *Owner:* as recommended. *Superseded by the decision review (§14):* the curve criterion is deleted, and the burst is judged by item 2's refinement criteria on both cores (W4).
5. **Item 8.** *Recommendation:* re-run `burnout_timing.jl` on a quiet machine before deciding it. Measured at a load of 15 to 16 it reads V-BO-01 at 1.16 to 2.28× and V-BO-03 at 1.45 to 2.74× the old core at matched accuracy, V-BO-04 at 0.56 to 0.62×, and two runs at that load differ by up to 0.9 in a ratio at 30 and 60 cells; at 120 cells the target, the old core's window RMS at reltol 1e-6, is no smaller than at 1e-5 (0.0087 K at both, C70's floor) and the new core reaches it at 3e-6 (0.022 K at 1e-5), and V-BO-03, whose layer stays activated to its end (C75), costs more than V-BO-01. Above 1.5× on a quiet machine, R40's levers come first. *Changes:* whether R40's levers move from the phase gate to before M5. *Owner:* M5 proceeds; `burnout_timing.jl` is re-run on a quiet machine by M6's item 8, and R40's levers come first only if it reads above 1.5×.
6. **For M6:** whether V-BO-03 runs long enough for item 1 to see its layer's invocation (C75); whether the caloric inversion should start from a guess the state alone determines (C79); V-CPL-02 under the correction (C80), which item 5 asks to stay inactive. *Owner:* V-BO-03's ladder rows run past 500 s until its layer's invocation, the case and its pinned QoIs unchanged; the warm start is kept (C79); item 5 splits by activation, asserting bit-identity and an activation of exactly zero where the correction stays inactive, and on the vanishing decks recording the activation and asserting that their QoIs pass.

**M5 is done** (§12.1): event location behind `located_events`, on by default since item 7 passes on every deactivating case (D78); the located crossing widens the threshold by the relative tolerance (D76, C81); V-BO-04's ledger closes across its event (D79); the heuristic restart stays (D80). The owner's questions from M5: item 5's identity decks that deactivate (C84), and D77's interval for a single invocation.

**The decision review restructures M6** (§14). The owner accepted its six recommendations, and §14 plans their implementation. M6 becomes M6a (the review's changes), M6b (the gate's cases) and M6c (one measurement pass on the final defaults), with the `FBDF` switch (MF) between M6b and M6c once an OrdinaryDiffEqBDF release contains the upstream fix. *Owner:* §14 approved, questions 16 to 24 as recommended; M6a is under way. The old M6's items are all in it:

- `burnout_gradients.jl` in M6a (W3);

W6's pre-release measurement on #4662's merge commit retires R43 for that fix (Appendix C.7). As of the M6a record no registered OrdinaryDiffEqBDF after 2.4.13 exists, #4662 is merged (`8d8191b`), and #4695 and #4687 are open. **Next: M6b** (§14.4): V-BO-05 re-pinned and routed (its finite-volume ladder: the `:interior` stop at 260.35, 259.79, 259.62 and 259.57 s at 30, 60, 120 and 240 cells, both forms within 5 ms, no event before it, the largest live temperature 693 K; proposed: the 240-cell time at 0.7 s, three times the CI's distance from it), V-BO-06, 07 and 08, and V-BO-03's ladder rows past 500 s. MF waits for the release (question 17: M6c waits at most a week after M6b, then measures on `Rodas5P`).

The owner's questions: V-BO-07's merged fraction (C91) and the old core's ledger on V-BO-06 (C92). At M6b's end no registered OrdinaryDiffEqBDF after 2.4.13 exists, and #4695 and #4687 are open. *Owner:* Phase 2 is to be completed now, so M6c and M7 run on `Rodas5P` (question 17's option (a)); MF becomes the first milestone after the release, before the cutover, and re-runs items 5, 8 and 9 and the suite then. **Next: M6c** (§14.4).

The decisions at M2's stop. The gate was met on V-BO-02 and not on V-BO-01, where the burnout path with the correction ran at 2.5 to 3.1× the old core at matched accuracy by D58's window maximum and 1.8× by the RMS of C70: above 2× on the gate's measure, so by §7.4 the phase stopped for the owner's decision. The recommendations, on the measurements of C68 to C71:

1. **The gas rows' tolerance (C60, C69).** *Recommendation:* keep `reltol · ε_m · mˢᵢ(0)`. No setting of the dial helps everywhere: tightening costs `Rodas5P` 2 to 2.5× on V-BO-02, where it resolves the venting after every event, buys 20 % on V-BO-01, nothing on the audit decks, and nowhere makes `KenCarp4` with the flag kept competitive. Keeping it moves no trajectory, so no digest is re-recorded and C60 closes. *Owner:* as recommended.
2. **The integrator of the burnout path and its restart (C61, C62, C65).** *Recommendation:* `Rodas5P` everywhere, with the heuristic restart after an event as a third restart kind of `IntegratorPolicy`. Under (1) `KenCarp4` needs the flag cleared, which costs it 20 to 60× its accuracy on the finite slab (C61), so it would be a second policy for deactivating solves alone, and it buys nothing at matched accuracy: V-BO-02 0.84× against `Rodas5P`'s 0.80 and 0.84×, V-BO-01 1.26 s at 3e-7 against 1.33 s at 1e-6 with C68's union. `Rodas5P`'s error converges with the tolerance where `KenCarp4`'s floors at 0.18 to 0.30 K (C70). The heuristic restart keeps V-BO-02's ledger within C39's 3e-3 at every tolerance (2.4e-5 to 9.3e-5, against 5.4e-3 and 4.4e-3 at reltol 1e-5 and 3e-6 with the solver-chosen restart), costs 0.04× at V-BO-02's matched tolerance and 0.19× at 1e-5, and wins at 1e-6 and below. *Owner:* as recommended.
3. **Whether M2 continues.** *Recommendation:* continue, on two measured levers, then re-measure and stop if they fall short. (a) The learned-union factorization of C68 as the package's sparse factorization on every layout: 16 to 22 % off V-BO-01, accuracy-neutral; it would also apply on V-BO-02 (8,704 stored entries against a union of 3,200) and on the audit decks, unmeasured. (b) The right-hand side: 26.7 µs per call in the solve against the old core's 15.9 (`derive!` about half of it, D60). With both, and the right-hand side at 18 µs, V-BO-01 projects to 1.3 to 1.5× matched by the RMS (`Rodas5P` at 1e-5) and 1.9 to 2.1× by the window maximum (`Rodas5P` at 1e-6), over the old core's 0.57 to 0.66 s. Then `burnout_timing.jl` is re-run with C70's measures beside D58's and the old default's time taken as a median over more solves. With it the owner decides the measure item 8 matches on (C70): the window maximum reads one sample at the old core's floor; the RMS reads the whole window. If V-BO-01 stays above 2× on the chosen measure after (a) and (b), the phase stops for the choice between the fallback (C71: `c = 0` halves the cost before the first event, but the interior merge is unbuilt, C54) and the cutover itself. No longer levers: the Jacobian's colors and chunk (C66), Jacobian reuse in a Rosenbrock-W method (A.7: the W-methods need 2 to 3× `Rodas5P`'s work at matched accuracy) and the gas tolerance (C69). *Owner:* as recommended, item 8 matched on the RMS; M2 continues with (a) and (b).

## 13. Owner's questions

Each with a recommendation and what the answer changes. Questions 1, 2, 4 and the V-BO-04 part of 8 are needed before M1 starts (§7.1); the rest by the milestone named. **Owner: every question decided as recommended** (the interior policy by the block-and-band rule with the slot-shifting merge as the fallback only; the band an absolute fraction of 0.1; `τ` the layer's equidistribution time set by the ladder; thresholds as `setup` keywords with the optional material field; event location on if item 7 passes; the wall-time ratio decided on item 8's table; the solver-chosen restart kept; the V-BO-04, V-BO-05, V-BO-03 and surface-Newton re-pins approved and the 30-cell V-BO-01 row decided after the tail diagnostic; the Douglas fir comparison at the cutover; the accuracy-per-tolerance investigation closed; the temperature form; the switch decided on R25's decomposition; the design errata at the design's next revision; radial Beer–Lambert deferred to Phase 4a; the Péclet weighting not in Phase 2).

1. **The interior policy on a moving mesh.** The block-and-band rule plus the correction is the primary path; the slot-shifting interior merge (C54, the old core's rule) is either insurance built now or the fallback built only if the correction fails its gate. *Recommendation:* the fallback only; the prototype carries V-BO-01, 03's deck and 04 without it, and it is a cross-cutting contract (every per-slot constant shifts) worth one and a half weeks. *Changes:* built now, M3 grows by one and a half weeks and the fallback of item 11 is already in hand; later, the schedule risk of §7.4 stays.
2. **The band.** An absolute near-threshold mass fraction `ε_band` (default 0.1) or a multiple of `ε_m`. *Recommendation:* absolute, 0.1, set by the ladder (a multiple took cells at 29 % of their mass at `ε_m = 3e-2`, A.4; three thresholds leave cells behind at 60 cells, A.3). *Changes:* the parameter's name and default in `setup` and in the design errata (§7.2).
3. **`τ`.** The layer's equidistribution time with the `N²` factor in the formula (§3.2), or the prototype's per-cell form. *Recommendation:* the layer time, a `setup` keyword whose default the ladder sets at constant `τ` across 30, 60 and 120 cells (the prototype's one-invocation run is `τ = 720` s at 60 cells, `T_sync` 73 s), with the heuristic that `T_sync = τ/π²` is at most a tenth of the layer's remaining life after full activation; the per-cell form makes `τ` mesh-dependent and is what produced the ladder's rows. *Changes:* R27 reduces to one physical ratio, `T_sync` against the layer's life, measured on V-BO-07 and V-BO-08.
4. **Thresholds as material parameters** (design question 9). `setup` keywords with package defaults plus an optional `Material` residual-thickness fraction (additive to the old core, fingerprint-checked), or package constants. *Recommendation:* the keywords and the optional field, since a char former that keeps 20 % of its thickness must not activate and only the material knows that. *Changes:* with the field, M1 edits Materials and runs the fingerprint and the old core's `Pkg.test()`; without it, charring decks rely on the activation never reaching 30 % (true of the current decks) and a new material could activate wrongly. *Owner (§14):* the optional material field is dropped (D69); the thresholds stay keywords of the correction (D65).
5. **Event location by default.** On (2 to 4 % of the solve; the event time tied to the tolerance, A.5) or off. *Recommendation:* on, if §7.3 item 7 passes with it; the discrete predicate stays as the safety net either way. *Changes:* the default of `located_events` and whether item 7's budget is met by the deactivating cases.
6. **The wall-time clause.** As written (no worse than the old core) or an exception for Phase 2. *Owner's decision:* the burnout path must run within the same order as the old core or faster; a path that cannot is a bad sign for the architecture. Written into the plan as milestone M2 (§5.7, §7): the integrator diagnosis and the Jacobian levers first, measured at matched accuracy with a cost breakdown, a pass at 1.5×, the owner's call between 1.5 and 2×, and a stop above 2× for the owner's decision on the phase and the cutover. *Changes:* M2 precedes the correction's build; the estimate grows by two to three weeks; the rest of the phase depends on M2's gate.
7. **The restart after an event** on deactivating solves. Solver-chosen (the default; V-BO-02's ledger quantity 1.5e-3 against C39's 3e-3) or forced `DT_AFTER_EVENT` (30 % more time, ledger 7.6e-5, A.6). *Recommendation:* keep solver-chosen and C39's tolerance; measure both on V-BO-01 with the correction at M5, and switch only if a QoI needs it. *Changes:* V-BO-02's ledger tolerance (back to 1e-3 under the forced restart) and the step budgets.
8. **Re-pins.** V-BO-04 per C46 (one invocation, one survivor); V-BO-05 per C45 (`:interior` at the first `ε_m` crossing, the time from a ladder); V-BO-03's warning QoI becomes the transmitted series' peak; the surface-Newton warning count of V-BO-01/03 becomes a count of clamped trial iterates once C36 removes the warnings, or is dropped; V-BO-01 at 30 PMMA cells if the end-of-layer spike (R25) persists after M4's diagnostic. *Recommendation:* approve the first three now (M3 needs V-BO-04's); decide V-BO-01's after the diagnostic, with the mechanism named. *Changes:* which QoIs the gate asserts and which it reports.
9. **The Douglas fir deck.** The both-cores comparison in Phase 2 or in Phase 3. *Recommendation:* Phase 2 runs it as the "charring deck identical" check (bit-identical with `NoCorrection`) and records both cores' times (A.8: 1.9× at equal tolerance, 49 colors); the matched-accuracy comparison on the real wood decks is the cutover's end-to-end table (design §6 Phase 3 item 6). *Changes:* whether M6 carries a matched-accuracy run of a 600 s deck (about a day of measurement) or Phase 3 does.
10. **The accuracy-per-tolerance investigation** carried from gate item 2. Close it with A.7 and A.9 (no candidate meets the old core's error within 1.5× of its time on the moving-mesh deck; the tolerance weights are not a lever; `Rodas5Pe` is the closest on the Darcy and Eulerian decks), or spend more. *Recommendation:* close it and carry the exception to Phase 3, where the cutover decision weighs it. *Changes:* a week of M5 if the owner wants the Rosenbrock error estimators studied further.
11. **The energy form for `c ≠ 0`.** Temperature (the gate's default) or the inventory form the design named for this work. *Recommendation:* temperature; the prototype's enthalpy run reproduces every QoI at 3 to 4× the time (A.4), and the ledgers close in both. *Changes:* which form the V-BO cases run in by default; the ladder runs both regardless.
12. **The reactivity switch** (C27). Keep at 1.5 `ε_m`, drop it under an `EquidistributionCorrection`, or trigger the band at its onset. *Recommendation:* decide on R25's decomposition measurement at M4; the hypothesis is that the switch is the release part of the end-of-layer spike and is no longer needed once the band deactivates cells at or above `ε_m`. *Changes:* a `setup` keyword either way; with the switch off C27's V-CPL-02 numbers are re-run. *Owner (§14):* deleted.
13. **The design errata of §7.2.** Apply them to the design document in M0 (a design revision commit) or at the design's next revision. *Recommendation:* at the next revision, listed here, since the owner owns the design text.
14. **Radial Beer–Lambert** (C37, carried open). *Recommendation:* deferred to Phase 4a, where the first radial case needs it; no burnout case is radial and `setup` refuses the combination (D45). *Changes:* nothing in Phase 2.
15. **The Péclet weighting of the Darcy upwind** (C21). *Recommendation:* not in Phase 2: the default integrator has no Newton to fail at the kink, and no Phase 2 deck reverses its flow. *Changes:* a week of M6 if the owner wants it measured on the audit decks now.

The questions raised by the decision review are §14.8's, numbered from 16.
